It is the safety net for Claude Code's own install of the packages, which may not have run (no Bun at the time, no network, or a plugin loaded in place from a local folder).

`mcpServers` registers one MCP server, `semantic-layer`: `bun "${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT}/skills/semantic-layer/scripts/mcp/server.ts" --role interviewer`. Claude Code starts it in the directory the session starts in, so its root is `SEMANTIC_ROOT`, else `./semantic` there, and names its tools `mcp__plugin_semantic-layer-builder_semantic-layer__semlayer_<command>`. It serves every command but `--show-protected`, and the hook above holds role agents to their roles.

## CLI

`semlayer <command> [flags]` where the plugin's `bin/semlayer` is on `PATH`, else `bun skills/semantic-layer/scripts/semlayer.ts <command> [flags]`. Every command takes `--root <dir>` (the semantic directory itself, the one that holds `semantic.yaml`), `--env-file <path>` (or `SEMLAYER_ENV_FILE`: the env file that holds the connection variables, see Connection variables and env files) and `--json`, and `semlayer <command> --help` prints its usage. Commands never print secrets and never write outside the semantic root, but for the env-file lines `semlayer init` and `semlayer setup` add to the `.gitignore` beside it, the `.env.example` `semlayer setup` writes there, and the new folder `semlayer demo` makes (see Demo project). Commands can run at once: one writes at a time, and each file is written whole (see Locking and whole-file writes). Every query a command runs is read-only by construction (see Trust model). Values printed from a database have control characters and ANSI escapes stripped. Every command that reads a database (`introspect`, `profile`, `check-join`, `goldens`) takes `--emit-sql`, `--results <file>` and `--plan <plan_id>` (see Query-plan protocol). A flag marked `...` repeats, and a repeated `--include`, `--exclude` or `--remove` value may hold several comma-separated globs; a value starting with `re:` is taken whole. The table has one single-line row per command form, sorted by its Command cell: a command with several forms has a row for each.

| Command | Does |
|---|---|
| `access [--user <json object of attributes>] [--user-file <path>] [--roles r1,r2] [--show visible\|hidden\|all]` | Explains what one user sees: the roles they hold and why (`assigned`, `condition`, `default`), and per source the visible and hidden models, fields and metrics with the reason each is hidden, and the row filters per model with their bound attribute values or `no rows` (see access.yaml). With no `--user` and no `--roles`, the default audience. A draft `access.yaml` is explained and labelled as a draft, naming the version that keeps applying when it has `last_approved`. An approved item that changed since its approval, or lost its hash while others carry one, is treated as a draft and named with the command that approves it again; `--json` lists them in `changed_since_approval` and `hash_missing` (see Checking access). Human output shows each source's visible counts on its own line under the totals line "Items visible across N source(s) (row filters limit rows, not these counts): ...", always prints each source's row filters section ("row filters: none (every row of every visible model)" when there are none), names assigned roles only when `--roles` was given, and shows hidden items by default; `--json` carries everything. |
| `add-attribute --name <n> --type string\|number\|string_list\|number_list [--description <text>]` | Appends a user attribute to `access.yaml`'s `user_attributes`. See Access edits. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, warnings, entry, attribute }`, plus `returned_to_draft` and `last_approved` as every access edit. |
| `add-bridge --source <s> --model <m> --name <join> --to <target model> --through <bridge model> --keys <left>=<bridge column>[,...] --through-keys <bridge column>=<target column>[,...] [--dedupe distinct_pairs\|equal_split\|weight] [--weight <bridge column>] [--type left\|inner] [--origin owner\|inferred] [--description <text>]` | Appends a draft bridge join (`status: draft`, `relationship: one_to_many`) to the model's `joins`: the target (`to`), the fact-to-bridge `keys`, and `through` with the bridge model, its `keys` to the target, and `dedupe` and `weight` when given (the owner chooses the dedupe rule; a draft may leave it unset, an approved join needs it). `--weight` goes only with `--dedupe weight` and names a numeric column of the bridge. Refuses a name a dimension, measure or join of the model already has; a target or bridge that is not a model of the source, or a bridge that is the model or the target; a column the snapshot's table lacks; a key or weight column in an `ignored_columns` list; and either hop across schemas no approved pair declares (`scope.cross_schema_undeclared`). Names `semlayer readback --targets <ref>` next, or, with no rule yet, what to ask the owner. See Typed edits. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, join, warnings, next }`. |
| `add-cross-schema --source <s> --from <schema> --to <schema> --decline --reply <owner's words>` | Records that the owner does not want the two schemas joined: a pair with `status: declined`, the owner's words (credentials redacted) in `reply` and today's date in `declined_at`, replacing a pair of the two schemas either way. A declined pair asks no more draft questions about keys between them. Takes no `--keys` or `--constraint`. See Schema scope, Schema pairs. `--json`: `{ changed, file, ref, pair, next }`. |
| `add-cross-schema --source <s> --from <schema> --to <schema> [--keys <table>.<column>=<table>.<column>[,...]] [--constraint <name>[,<name>]]` | Records a draft schema pair in the source's `cross_schema`, from the schema holding the foreign key to the schema it references, with at least one key: `--keys` pairs a column of a table in `--from` with one of a table in `--to`, and `--constraint` takes the columns of a foreign key the snapshot names. Refuses a key or constraint the snapshot lacks, a schema that is not among the schemas the source reads (naming the `semlayer setup --schemas` command that reads it), the same schema twice, and a pair the source already declares the other way. Keys added to a recorded pair are merged and send it back to draft; the same keys again change nothing. Names `semlayer approve --target schema-pair:<s>:<from>:<to>` next, on the owner's yes. See Schema scope, Schema pairs. `--json`: `{ changed, file, ref, pair, next }`. |
| `add-field <source.model> --kind dimension\|measure --name <n> --expr <e> [--type time\|string\|number\|boolean] [--agg sum\|count\|count_distinct\|avg\|min\|max] [--filter <f>] [--description <text>] [--set key=value]... [--unignore]` | Appends a draft dimension or measure (always `status: draft`) to the model's `dimensions` or `measures`, its keys in the JSON Schema's order. A dimension needs `--expr` and `--type`; a measure needs `--agg`, and `--expr` unless `--agg count`. `--set key=value`, repeatable, sets any other key the schema allows (`time_grains`, `time_zone`, `format`, `currency`, `synonyms`, `tags`), its value read as `set` reads one; the approval fields and `name` are refused. Refuses a name a dimension, measure or join of the model already has; an expr that is one bare or quoted column the snapshot's table lacks; and a field reading a column in `ignored_columns`, naming `ignore-column --remove`, unless `--unignore`, which removes the model's own column from `ignored_columns` in the same edit (a column of a model a join reference reaches is refused even so; see Ignored columns in typed edits). A computed expr is written, with a warning naming the names in it that are not columns of the table. A field tagged `pii` or `sensitive` over a column whose profile holds sample values names `semlayer profile --source <s> --redact`. See Typed edits. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, kind, field, unignored, warnings, redact }`. |
| `add-join --source <s> --model <m> --name <join> --to <model> --keys <left>=<right>[,<left>=<right>] [--relationship many_to_one\|one_to_one\|one_to_many] [--type left\|inner] [--origin owner\|inferred] [--description <text>]` | Appends a draft join (always `status: draft`) to the model's `joins`: `name`, `to`, `keys` (one `{ from, to }` per pair, a repeated pair written once), `relationship` (default `many_to_one`), `type` when given, `origin` (default `owner`, the owner stated it; `inferred` for one guessed from names or values) and `description`; a missing or empty `joins` list becomes a block list. Refuses a name a dimension, measure or join of the model already has; a target that is not a model of the source; a key column the snapshot's table lacks (each is written as the snapshot spells it; a table the snapshot lacks is written with a warning); and a key column in either model's `ignored_columns`, naming `ignore-column --remove`. Names `semlayer check-join --source <s> --model <m> --join <join>` next, then the readback. See Typed edits. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, join, warnings, next }`. |
| `add-metric --name <n> --type ratio\|derived [--source <s>] [--numerator <model.measure> --denominator <model.measure>] [--expr <e>] [--format number\|integer\|currency\|percent] [--currency <code>] [--description <text>] [--set key=value]...` | Appends a draft metric (always `status: draft`) to `metrics.yaml`, its keys in the JSON Schema's order, creating the file when there is none. `--source` defaults to `semantic.yaml`'s `default_source`, else the only source. A ratio needs `--numerator` and `--denominator`, each `model.measure`, and takes no `--expr`; a derived metric needs `--expr`, which the reference compiler must read as `{model.measure}` placeholders with numbers and `+ - * / ( )`, and takes neither side. `--set key=value`, repeatable, sets `synonyms` or `tags` (any other key the schema allows), its value read as `set` reads one; the approval fields are refused. Refuses a name `metrics.yaml` already has, and, as `semlayer validate` reads the result, a measure that does not resolve in the source. Names the draft measures it uses, which need approving before the metric can be. See Typed edits. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, metric, draft_measures, created }`. |
| `add-metric --none --by <owner> --text <owner's words>` | Records the owner's answer that they report no metrics for now in `metrics.yaml`'s `no_metrics`: `by` and `text` from `--by` and `--text` (each folded to one line, credentials redacted) and today's date in `at`, creating the file when there is none; recording it again replaces it. Takes no other flag. Refuses without `--by` or `--text` (code `owner_words_missing`, `next` this command), and while `metrics.yaml` lists a metric, naming them. `semlayer apply` refuses it as an entry: it records an owner's answer. See metrics.yaml, No metrics for now. `--json`: `{ changed, file, no_metrics, created, next }`. |
| `add-restriction --name <n> --targets <ref>,... [--roles <role>,...] [--when <condition>] [--description <text>]` | Appends a restriction to `access.yaml`'s `restrictions`; at least one of `--roles` and `--when` is given. Refuses a target that matches no metric, model or field, which `semlayer validate` only warns about in a file. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, warnings, entry, restriction }`. |
| `add-role --name <n> [--description <text>] [--assign-when <condition>] [--allow-models <p>,...] [--allow-tags <t>,...] [--deny-models <p>,...] [--deny-fields <source.model.field>,...] [--deny-tags <t>,...]` | Appends a role to `access.yaml`'s `roles`. A condition that cannot be read is refused with the condition syntax, which `--help` prints too. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, warnings, entry, role }`. |
| `add-row-filter --role <r> --model <source.model> (--expr <predicate> \| --column <c> --via <source.model> --manager <c> --member <c> --user <attribute> [--exclude-self])` | Appends a row filter to a role's `row_filters`: an `expr`, or a `hierarchy` (written with `user: user.<attribute>` and `include_self`, false with `--exclude-self`). Refuses an `expr` that names a column the model's table lacks in the snapshot, as `check-rule` reads it, with the closest names. The same filter again changes nothing. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, warnings, filter, role }`. |
| `add-segment --source <s> --model <m> --name <segment> --filter <sql predicate> [--description <text>] [--replace]` | Adds a segment to the model's `segments` (a name that matches `^[a-z][a-z0-9_]*$`, the filter folded to one line), checked as a measure filter is before anything is written: one read-only expression over the model's own columns and join references, no subquery, no ignored column, and no dotted name that is neither a column nor a join (`expr.*` codes of Expression text). A bare name is not judged, since a keyword and a column cannot be told apart by name; the output names the `semlayer check-rule` command that runs the filter against the database. A name the model already declares is refused as `segment_exists`, naming `--replace`; `--replace` rewrites the filter (and the description when given) of a declared one in place, and a name it does not declare is refused as `segment_missing`. When an approved measure names the replaced segment, its hash changes as `set` of its own filter would: the item stays as written, is listed in `reapprove` and in one line each by name, and is read back and approved again on the owner's yes (see Segments). `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, segment, replaced, measures, affected, redact, next }`; `measures` are the refs of the measures that name it and `affected` those of them that are approved. See Typed edits. |
| `add-source --name <s> --dialect <d> [--env VAR] [--database <path>] [--view name=relation]... [--setup <statement>]... [--schemas a,b] [--description <text>]` | Writes `sources/<s>.yaml`. `--env` is required for postgres and refuses anything that looks like a URL or a credential; a note says when neither the environment nor an env file sets it. `--schemas` writes `intake.schemas` with `intake.mode: connection`, refusing a name that is not a schema name or repeats one; its next hint passes them on, single-quoted when one holds `$`, and `--json` lists them as `schemas`. |
| `apply --file <path \| ->` | Runs several typed edits as one, under one lock, with one validation at the end: all of them land or none do (see Batches of edits). The file, or stdin with `-`, holds `{"edits": [[...], ...]}`, each entry the words of one typed edit after `semlayer`. Prints each entry's own result line with, under it, each warning `semlayer validate` gives that the entry added (found by running the entries again, each validated on its own, only when the batch added a warning; one only the entries together add is printed after them), then the approved items the batch changed, once, with the readback command. A refused entry does not stop the check: every entry is parsed and run, each seeing the ones before, and one refusal names every refused entry with its number, its words and its own message (`semlayer validate`'s for a value that can never be right, see Typed edits), and nothing is written. An entry that names what a refused entry would have created (a field, join, metric, role, restriction, attribute or golden it adds, or a new name it gives) is listed as not checked, "Entries <n>, ... were not checked: they read what entry <m> would have created", in place of its own error. A single refused entry keeps its own message, "Entry <n> (<words>) failed, so nothing was written: ...". An error only the final validation finds is named by the entry that adds it, as before. `--json`: `{ applied: [{ entry, command, result, warnings? }], reapprove, warnings, warning_issues }`, `warnings` on an entry being the lines it added, the batch's `warnings` every line the batch added and `warning_issues` the same warnings as `{ code, file, path, message }`, `result` being the entry's own `--json` output with an empty `reapprove` (the batch's own `reapprove` lists them), plus `returned_to_draft: true` and `last_approved` when the batch made an approved `access.yaml` a draft. |
| `approve --date <YYYY-MM-DD>` | Refused: an approval's date comes from the owner's recorded reply. |
| `approve --readback <readback_id> [--by <name>]` | Takes each item of the readback (or each `--target`) on its own, exactly as `--target <ref>` does, models and fields first, then metrics, then `access`: it approves each one whose owner's latest reply is a yes to it as it now stands, from that reply, and lists every other with why (a change, a no, no reply, no verdict, changed since the yes, `--by` naming someone else, a check that fails) and the command that settles it. A readback's goldens are left to `goldens confirm --readback`. Exits 0 when every item whose latest reply is a yes was approved, and 1 when one of them was not; an item the owner did not say yes to is listed and does not fail it. Takes no `--recursive` or `--revoke`. `--json`: `{ readback_id?, changed, approved, not_approved, warnings, all_yes_approved }`, `approved` holding `{ target, item?, approved, already?, by, at, ledger, from? }` and `not_approved` `{ target, item?, reason, next, said_yes }`. |
| `approve --stamp-hashes [--target <ref>] [--confirm-unchanged]` | Records `approved_hash` on every approved item that has none (or those under `--target`: a model and its fields, a field, a metric or `access`), as it now stands, keeping `approved_by` and `approved_at`; takes no `--by`, `--date`, `--revoke` or `--recursive`. A `--target` that names nothing is an error. While other items carry a hash it refuses the items without one unless `--confirm-unchanged` is given (see Approval hashes). Needs no reply in the ledger. `--json`: `{ stamped: [refs] }`. |
| `approve --target <ref> --target <ref> ... [--by <name>]` | Takes each `--target` on its own, as `approve --readback` takes each item of a readback: the same order, reasons, exit status and `--json`, without `readback_id`. Takes no `--recursive` or `--revoke`. |
| `approve --target <ref> [--by <name>] [--recursive] [--revoke]` | Marks an item approved on the owner's recorded yes, or draft again with `--revoke`. `<ref>` is `source.model`, `source.model.field`, `metric:<name>`, `access` or `schema-pair:<source>:<from>:<to>` (see Schema scope, Approving a schema pair); goldens use `goldens confirm`. Refuses, writing nothing, unless the owner's latest reply to the target in `.semlayer/ledger.jsonl` is a yes whose `content_hash` equals the target's current hash; `approved_by` and `approved_at` come from that reply, and a `--by` that names someone else is refused (see Approval requires a recorded yes). `--recursive` approves the items that have their own yes, lists the others with `semlayer readback --targets ...` and exits 1. `--revoke` needs no reply. Records `approved_hash` (see Approval hashes); an approved item that changed since is approved again, one that did not is left as it is. `--revoke` removes `approved_hash`, and on `access` moves `approved_by` and `approved_at` into `last_approved: { by, at }`; approving it again removes `last_approved`. Warns on a join with no evidence, on a to-one join whose current evidence shows fan-out without `fanout_override`, and on a join whose evidence shows `many_to_many`, which needs a bridge table whatever `fanout_override` says. `--json`: `{ changed, warnings, last_approved?, refused? }`, `refused` holding `{ target, reason, next }` for each item it did not approve. |
| `baseline --source <s> [--tables t,...] [--out <file>] [--full]` | Read-only. Writes a first-pass brief of the source from its snapshot (see Baseline brief): per table its probable role, grain, keys, relationships, candidate measures and splits, columns that should not be splits and questions for the owner, and a star-schema sketch of the source. Plain output is markdown; `--json` is scoped to the phase `semlayer next` names (Baseline brief, Scoped JSON) and `--full` carries the whole structure; `--out` writes the markdown to a path resolved from the semantic root and refuses one outside it. |
| `brief --source <s> (--batch <n> \| --tables t,...) [--budget <tokens>]` | Read-only. Prints one interview batch as a compact packet for a batch analyst (see Batch packets): batch `n` of the tables `semlayer status` would interview, `batch_size` at a time (batch 1 is `next_batch`), or the `--tables` named, each with its columns and counts, drafted fields, joins, open questions and baseline guesses, trimmed in a fixed order to `--budget` tokens (default 6,000, estimated as characters / 4). Data text is fenced as `{ text, from }`; protected columns carry counts only. `--json`: `{ source, batch, batches, tables, budget: { limit, used, truncated, within_limit, dropped } }`. |
| `change close --version <major.minor>` | Records a release in `changelog.yaml`: the first with no changelog, or the open round's; sets `layer_version` and removes `.semlayer/change.json` (see Closing a round). A draft never approved and not in the last release is left out of the release and listed (`drafts_left_out`); it is refused, writing nothing, while an approved item changed since its approval is not approved again (or an item the last release held is now a draft), a confirmed golden reads a left-out draft, `semlayer validate` has an error or an affected golden's rerun figures differ from its recorded rows (`release.close_blocked`, each blocker with its command); a golden on a source that cannot be queried is listed as not rerun. A close that finishes an already-recorded release prints `{ closed, version, already_recorded: true, layer_version, next }` under `--json`. Also refuses a missing, malformed or not-higher version (`release.close_missing_version`, `release.version_invalid`, `release.version_not_above`) and a released layer with no round (`release.close_no_round`). `--json`: `{ closed, version, date, note, file, items, added, changed, removed, goldens_moved, goldens_unconfirmed, goldens_removed, not_rerun, drafts_left_out, layer_version, next }`; a golden the last release recorded that is a draft now or gone is left out and named (`goldens_unconfirmed`, `goldens_removed`). |
| `change start --note <text> --by <name>` | Opens a change round on a released layer: writes `.semlayer/change.json` with the note, who asked, when (the command's clock) and the release it starts from (see The change round). Refuses a layer with no release (`release.change_unreleased`, naming `semlayer change close --version 1.0`), a second round (`release.change_open`, naming `semlayer change status`), a missing or empty `--note` or `--by` (`release.change_missing_input`), a too-long note or name (`release.change_too_long`) and a `change.json` that does not read (`release.change_malformed`). `--json`: `{ started, file, version, note, started_by, started_at, from_version, drafts_at_start, next }`. |
| `change status` | Read-only. Lists the items added, changed and removed since the last entry of `changelog.yaml`, each changed item marked `approved` (approved again at its new content) or `draft` (waiting for the owner's yes) with the approved goldens that read it, and quotes `.semlayer/drift.json` when present (see Change status). A layer with no changelog exits 0 and names `semlayer change close --version 1.0`. `--json`: one object `{ released, version, date?, added, changed: [{ ref, state, affected_goldens }], removed, added_drafts, drift, next }`, `added_drafts` the added refs that are drafts (shown `(draft)` in the plain output); a draft never approved is added, waits for nothing, and `next` offers the close only for a draft the owner deferred (see The change round). |
| `check-join --source <s> (--model <m> --join <j> \| --model <m> --all \| --all) [--no-write]` | Measures a join's match rate, key uniqueness and fan-out and writes `evidence`, nothing else; for a bridge join, also the bridge's counts under `evidence.through` (see Bridges and multi-fact requests). Works join by join: a join whose query the database refuses is skipped with its reason and the others are written (see Skipped tables). |
| `check-rule --source <s> --model <m> (--filter <expr> \| --dimension-expr <expr>) [--emit-sql \| --results <file> \| --plan <plan_id>]` | Says whether the layer can express a row-level filter or a dimension expr on a model (its own columns and join references of one or two to-one hops; no aggregate, subquery or other table) and, when it can, counts `rows_matched` of `rows_total` with one read-only query, or says why not with a suggestion (see Reference compiler, Checking a rule); exits 1 when it cannot. A rule that reads a column in `ignored_columns` is refused before any query, naming `ignore-column --remove` (see Ignored columns in typed edits). Writes no file but the plan `--emit-sql` stores. |
| `demo [--dir <dir>]` | Makes a sample project to try semlayer on with no warehouse (see Demo project): `<dir>` (default `./semlayer-demo`) with `data/` (the synthetic Acme files, copied byte for byte), `semantic/` (one duckdb source over them, set up as `setup` does) and `OWNER.md`. Needs no network, connection, secret or project, and writes no env file. Refuses, changing nothing, when `<dir>` exists and is not empty, is inside a semantic root, or the plugin has no sample data; `--root` is ignored (the root is `<dir>/semantic`). `--json`: `{ dir, data: { dir, files }, root, project, source, tables, owner_sheet, synthetic, next: { cd, run, type, say } }` (`type` is `/semlayer`, the skill's slash command). |
| `doctor` | Read-only, writes nothing; needs no project. Checks, one line each with a mark (`[ok]`, `[warn]`, `[fail]`, `[--]` for not yet or not applicable), that semlayer can run here: Bun's version against 1.4.0; the packages installed (looked for in `node_modules` on disk, not through Bun's auto-install; DuckDB's native module loading); the project (a semantic root here, or not yet); for each source, where its connection comes from by variable and file name only (an unset one names the line to add, and the `.env.example` to copy when that file sets each variable), then one `select 1` through the connection (statement timeout 5 seconds, 15 seconds in all), and, on postgres, whether the role can only read (the role report of Setup, with `roleNote`'s fix); whether git keeps each env file the lookup reads out of version control (tracked: `git rm --cached`; not ignored: the `.gitignore` lines); and whether a bare `semlayer` on this `PATH`, read as it is, is the plugin's own (see Hand edits, Which command is semlayer). Each line that is not ok names its fix. It prints once the line that puts the plugin's `bin/` on a person's own `PATH`, then the permission rule `Bash(semlayer *)` a person may add under `permissions.allow` in `.claude/settings.local.json`, `.claude/settings.json` or `~/.claude/settings.json` so Claude Code stops asking before each `semlayer` command (it never adds the rule), then the next step `semlayer next` names. Never prints a value of a connection variable. Exits 1 only when Bun or the packages stop semlayer from running. `--json`: `{ ok, checks: [{ check, status, text, fix? }], shell_line, permission_rule: { rule, files: [{ file, scope }] }, next: { command, why } \| null }`. |
| `draft --source <s> --new-columns [--tables t,...]` | For each existing model on an in-scope table (or on `--tables`), drafts every new column (one no model over the same table reads or lists in `ignored_columns`, and no other model's field reads through a join or row filter reads) as a new draft dimension or measure, by the rules for a new model, with an open question "New column <c> (<type>) appeared in <table>; ...". A new foreign-key column whose target table is in scope or modeled becomes a draft join (`origin: declared` or `inferred`) instead of being left out; the currency question is asked only when no existing measure of the model has a `currency`. Appends to the model file, keeping comments and approved items byte-identical, and never changes the model's status; works whatever `focus_threshold`. `--json`: `{ source, updated: [{ model, added: [field names], skipped: [{ column, reason }] }], redacted: [table.column] }`, `redacted` being the protected columns of the updated models' tables whose snapshot profile it cut to counts only. |
| `draft --source <s> [--tables t,...] [--strip-prefix p,...] [--force]` | Writes draft models for in-scope tables that have none, with inferred keys and joins. Never overwrites a model. Refuses more tables than `focus_threshold` without `--tables` or `--force`. `--strip-prefix` is saved as `draft.strip_prefixes`. A count of rows and a count of a key column's distinct values get a factual description ("Number of rows of <table>", "Number of different <column> values") unless the column has a comment; a measure that carries a business rule gets none, for the owner's words. Tags a field `pii` when its column's name looks like personal data and `sensitive` when it looks like pay, banking, card, health, credential or free-text data, each with an open question (see Profiles of protected columns for the names). Leaves out a string column that is not a key or a code-like column and has a different value on nearly every row (more than 90% distinct over at least 50 rows), with an open question, and lists every column it leaves out in `ignored_columns`. The columns its models protect (a tagged field's, an ignored one) keep counts only in the snapshot from then on, with no `semlayer profile --redact` to run; `--json` lists them as `redacted`. A date or timestamp column gets a time dimension even inside a composite primary key; a timestamp without a time zone gets an open question asking which zone it is stored in, for `time_zone`. Money columns follow the baseline's rules (currency-code suffixes, `numeric(p,2)`), and a column or table comment naming a currency code turns the currency question into a confirmation. A table of 2 or 3 columns whose names all end in `_id`, such as a closure or bridge table, gets every column as a dimension, `primary_key` all of them and no `count_distinct` measures, with an open question asking the owner to confirm each combination appears once. Joins stay within a table's schema; a declared foreign key into another schema drafts no join and an open question (see Schema scope), and the output lists each such key; `--json` adds `cross_schema_keys: [{ table, columns, references, constraint }]`, `constraint` null for a key whose name the snapshot does not record. On a postgres source whose tables in scope are in more than one schema it refuses with `scope.primary_unset` until `intake.primary` is set, then drafts by default only the primary schema's tables and those an approved pair names, and `--json` adds `left_out_schemas: [{ schema, tables, command }]` (see Schema scope, The schema question). |
| `export-sql --dialect postgres\|redshift\|mysql\|sqlserver\|snowflake\|bigquery\|databricks\|duckdb [--schemas a,b] [--include <p>]... [--exclude <p>]...` | Prints that dialect's schema export query (see Schema exports), with its schema filter applied when `--schemas` is given, each name written as a SQL string literal with its quotes escaped (BigQuery: each dataset as a backtick-quoted identifier in its per-dataset block, and a name holding a backtick is refused); then, on stderr, one line saying how to run it and import the result (`semlayer import --source <s> --file schema.csv`, or `schema.tsv` for mysql). `--include` and `--exclude` write a table filter into the query where the dialect can express the pattern, and list any other in the query's header and on stderr with the `semlayer import --include/--exclude` that applies it (see Table filters). `--json`: `{ dialect, sql, run_hint }`, plus `filters` with `--include` or `--exclude`. Reads no database and needs no project. |
| `goldens check [--name <g>] [--source <s>] [--show-protected]` | Rechecks every approved golden (a golden the compiler refuses fails, with the reason and the step that resolves it; see goldens). `--show-protected`, and a result with two columns of one name, are as for `goldens run`. |
| `goldens confirm --name <g> [--by <name>] [--tolerance <t>] [--force] [--show-protected]` | Records a golden's rows on the owner's recorded yes (see goldens). On a golden whose source is an export source it refuses, writing nothing, as the `goldens run` row says; otherwise it refuses, writing nothing, unless the owner's latest reply to `golden:<g>` in `.semlayer/ledger.jsonl` is a yes to the golden with the rows its request compiles to now and the tolerance it records; `confirmed_by` and `confirmed_at` come from that reply, and a `--by` that names someone else is refused (see Confirming a golden requires a recorded yes). It prints the draft fields the request uses, then records the rows with the tolerance agreed with the owner (0 for figures finance signs off; without the flag the tolerance the owner's yes was read back with, else the golden's recorded tolerance, or 0.005); rerunning it with a new `--tolerance` records the rows again, and it records `confirmed_as` and warns when the golden's role is defined only in a draft `access.yaml`. `--force` only lifts the 200-row limit. Currency, `--show-protected` and a result with two columns of one name are as for `goldens run`. |
| `goldens confirm --readback <readback_id> [--by <name>] [--force] [--show-protected]` | Confirms each golden of that readback as `goldens confirm --name` does, at the tolerance it was read back with, and lists every other with why and the command that settles it; it exits 1 only when a golden the owner said yes to was not confirmed, and takes no `--name`, `--tolerance`, `--results`, `--plan` or `--emit-sql` (`--json`: `{ readback_id, confirmed, not_confirmed, all_yes_confirmed }`). `--force` only lifts the 200-row limit. `--show-protected` is as for `goldens run`. |
| `goldens new --name <g> --question <q> --request <json\|file> [--source <s>] [--role <r>] [--user <json>]` | Writes a draft golden and prints the SQL its request compiles to (see goldens); a request the compiler refuses for its own shape is refused, writing nothing (`golden.request_invalid`). |
| `goldens remove --name <g>` | Removes a draft golden's file; a confirmed one is refused (`golden.confirmed`), naming `goldens unconfirm` (see goldens). |
| `goldens run --name <g> [--show-protected]` | Compiles a golden's request from the current YAML and runs it with its `cross_check_sql` (see goldens). `confirm`, and `run` without `--emit-sql`, `--results` or `--plan`, refuse, writing nothing, on a golden whose source is an export source (`intake.mode: export`, decision D2: its rows come from the data), and `run` from stored rows ends with a hint in place of the confirm steps; the refusals and the hint each end "Once a connection exists: semlayer setup --source <s> --dialect <d> --env <VAR>, then semlayer goldens confirm --name <g>." (the wording `semlayer reply` and `semlayer review apply` use) when the owner's latest reply to `golden:<g>` is a yes, to any version of the golden (confirm rechecks it against the rows), else "..., then semlayer goldens run --name <g>.". `run` and `confirm` display a measure or metric with `format: currency` to two decimals; the recorded rows keep the values the query returns. Each of `run`, `confirm` and `check` takes `--show-protected`: without it a protected column's values print as `[protected]`, as in `semlayer run`, in rows and in differences, with the rows of `cross_check_sql` masked by what its statement reads (see Protected values in results), and the skill never passes it. A result with two columns of one name is refused with a message to alias them. A golden's `cross_check_sql` is code the command runs: review `goldens/` like code. |
| `goldens unconfirm --name <g> [--reason <why>]` | Returns a confirmed golden to draft, its recorded rows and confirmation removed and its tolerance kept, with no owner's yes needed (see goldens). Refuses a draft (`golden.not_confirmed`) or an unknown name (`golden.unknown`). |
| `ignore-column --source <s> --model <m> --column <c> [--reason <text>] [--remove]` | `--remove` takes the column out of `ignored_columns` (the key goes with its last entry) and adds no field; a column not listed changes nothing. Otherwise adds a column to the model's `ignored_columns`, spelled as in the snapshot when it has the table (a column it lacks is refused), before the first entry that sorts after it, with `--reason` as a comment on the entry; a column already listed, in any case, changes nothing. Refuses a column a dimension, measure, join key, the primary key or another model's join reads (the rules `semlayer validate` checks `ignored_columns` with): a field's column needs the field removed first. A `--reason` on a flow list turns it into a block list, its trailing comment moving to its own line under the key. When the snapshot holds sample values of the column, now protected, it names `semlayer profile --source <s> --redact`. A model without `ignored_columns` gets it after `open_questions`, or before its fields. See Typed edits. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, column, ignored_columns, reason?, redact }`; with `--remove`, `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, column, ignored_columns, removed: true }`. |
| `import --source <s> [--format information_schema\|dbt_manifest\|ddl] [--file <path>] [--catalog <catalog.json>] [--include <p>]... [--exclude <p>]... [--semantic [--emit <file>]]` | Builds the snapshot from shared context instead of a live connection: an information_schema.columns export or a schema export (see Schema exports) as CSV or a JSON array, a dbt `manifest.json` with an optional `catalog.json`, or a `.sql` file of DDL. `--format` is optional: a `.sql` file is `ddl`, a JSON object with `nodes` is `dbt_manifest`, and a CSV or a JSON array is `information_schema`; an explicit `--format` overrides, and a file that fits none is an error naming the formats. A DDL file is untrusted input: it is read in a DuckDB with external access off, extension autoinstall and autoload off and its configuration locked, and its `CREATE TABLE ... AS` and views get their columns without reading any data (`LIMIT 0` or the declared types). `--file` resolves from the working directory; left out, an export source imports its `intake.export.file`, resolved from the semantic root and refused outside it or when missing. When `intake.schemas` lists schemas, every format keeps only the tables of those schemas, matched as introspect matches them (a BigQuery dataset written alone also matches it under any project, and a DuckDB schema prefixed with the database the source opens matches the schema alone; a table with no schema matches none), and reports the rest: "Dropped 12 tables in 2 schemas not in intake.schemas: a, b"; when none is left it is an error naming the file's schemas. `--json` adds `dropped: { tables, schemas }` (`schemas` sorted, `null` for tables with no schema; zero and `[]` when nothing was dropped). `--include` and `--exclude` (the Scope patterns grammar, as `semlayer export-sql` names them for patterns its query could not hold) then keep only the tables, of those `intake.schemas` keeps, that match an include and no exclude, before the snapshot is compared and saved: it prints how many it kept and dropped, warns on a pattern that matches no table in the file ("within intake.schemas" when that list dropped some), and refuses a selection that keeps none; `--json` adds `filters: { include, exclude, kept, dropped }`, counted after the `intake.schemas` drop, which stays in `dropped`. Prints and reports `changes` as `introspect` does. `--semantic` (a dbt manifest only; else refused with code `semantic_needs_dbt_manifest`) also reports the manifest's semantic models and metrics without writing the layer: counts, join candidates and the skipped list with a reason each, and under `--json` a `semantic` object (see dbt semantic import). `--emit <file>` (with `--semantic` only) also writes the draft edits for the models already drafted, for `semlayer apply --file` (see dbt semantic import). |
| `init --name <name> [--description <text>] [--default-source <s>]` | Writes `semantic.yaml` and the directory layout, and adds `.env`, `.env.*` and `!.env.example` to the `.gitignore` of the directory that holds the root (see Connection variables and env files). |
| `introspect --source <s> [--schemas a,b]` | Captures tables, columns, keys and row estimates into the snapshot. Reads the schemas the source's `intake.schemas` lists when it lists any (an empty or absent list reads every schema; a BigQuery source with neither reads the datasets its scope's includes name, the `scope.tables_file` entries included), and keeps only their tables in the snapshot, leaving out (and counting) any other schema's tables a `--results` file holds. `--schemas` reads the given schemas, or BigQuery datasets, instead, keeping other schemas' tables from the previous snapshot; when the list differs from `intake.schemas` it prints so, with how to save it (stderr with `--emit-sql`). The same schemas filter the `--emit-sql` plan. `--json` adds `schemas` (the list read, `[]` for every schema), `schemas_from` (`flag`, `intake`, `scope` or `all`), `dropped: { tables, schemas }` (the tables left out, as `import` reports them) and, when they differ, `schemas_note`. The lists are compared as the dialect compares schema names, so `ANALYTICS` and `analytics` are the same Snowflake schema. Prints what changed since the previous snapshot; `--json` adds it as `changes` (see snapshots). On a postgres connection `--json` adds `role_report` (see Setup, The role report). Its next hint is `semlayer profile --source <s>` only when some in-scope table is not profiled; otherwise `semlayer validate` when modeled tables changed, or `semlayer status`. |
| `next` | Read-only, writes nothing. Prints the one next action (see semlayer next --json): approve what the owner said yes to, put a pending readback to the owner, dispatch a query plan that waits on results, else `semlayer status`'s next step. Plain output is one or two lines naming the command. |
| `profile --source <s> --redact` | Rewrites the existing snapshot's profiles of protected columns that way, without querying the database, and removes hashed top values earlier versions stored. |
| `profile --source <s> [--tables t,...] [--all] [--refresh]` | Profiles in-scope tables into the snapshot, table by table: a table the database refuses is skipped with its reason and the rest are saved (see Skipped tables). A run takes the tables that have no profile (`profiled_at` unset) and those the last run skipped; `--all`, or `--refresh`, profiles every table again. Stores counts only for a protected column: no top values, raw or hashed, and a `min` and `max` only for a number or a date, with `redacted: true` (see Profiles of protected columns). Over `profile.sample_rows`, a table is read through one sample (`TABLESAMPLE SYSTEM` on Postgres and BigQuery), and all its top values come from one query over it (see Sampling); a BigQuery profile prints a cost warning first. |
| `question add <ref> --text <text>` | Appends an open question to a model's `open_questions` (for `source.model.field`, the question starts `<field>: `), to the top-level `open_questions` of `access.yaml` for `access`, or to one role's for `access.roles.<role>` (an unknown role is refused, listing the roles); the same question again changes nothing. A model without the key gets it before `ignored_columns` or its fields. Neither list is part of an approval hash, so an approved model or access file stays approved. See Typed edits. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, question, open_questions }`. |
| `question list [<ref>]` | Lists the open questions of the layer, or of one source, model, field (the model's questions that start `<field>: `), `access` (its own and every role's) or `access.roles.<role>`, each with its target (the model, `access` or `access.roles.<role>` that holds it) and its 1-based index in that list, the two that a `question resolve --answers` entry takes; writes nothing. Prints `<target> #<index>: <question>` per question and the `question resolve --answers -` that records the answers. `--json`: `{ questions: [{ target, index, question }], count }`. |
| `question resolve --answers <file \| ->` | Resolves every question one owner turn answered: the file, or stdin with `-`, holds `{"by": "<owner>", "answers": [{"target": "<ref>", "match": "<text>" \| "index": <n>, "text": "<the owner's answer>"}, ...]}`, each entry picked as `--match` or `--index` picks it, an `index` counting in each list as it stands before this command. Every entry is checked first (its ref and question exist, the pick is one question, no question twice, an answer); on any error nothing is written and the message names the entry. Then each file's questions go in one validated edit and one `kind: question` line per entry is appended in one write, each the line the single form writes; a failed append puts the files back. `--json`: `{ changed, files, reapprove, ids, ledger_path, entries, redacted }`. |
| `question resolve <ref> (--index <n> \| --match <text>) --text <text> --by <owner>` | Removes one open question and records it with the owner's answer as a `kind: question` line of `.semlayer/ledger.jsonl` (see .semlayer/ledger.jsonl). `<ref>` is written as for `question add`: for `source.model.field` the candidates are the model's questions that start `<field>: `; for `access`, the top-level `open_questions`; for `access.roles.<role>`, that role's. `--index` picks the n-th candidate, counting from 1; `--match` the one candidate that contains the text, ignoring case, and more or fewer than one is an error listing the candidates numbered. The removal keeps every other byte of the file, a list it empties is removed with its key, and the file changes as a typed edit does (see Typed edits). The ledger is checked before the file is edited, the line is appended after the edited file validates and is written, and a failed append puts the file back, so a failure changes neither. `--text` is stored as `semlayer reply` stores it, credentials redacted. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, question, open_questions, ledger_id, ledger_path, entry, redacted }`. |
| `readback --discard <readback_id>` | Sets aside a pending readback the owner will not answer: it removes `.semlayer/pending/<readback_id>.json` (only a plain file inside the semantic root), leaving the empty marker `.semlayer/pending/<readback_id>.discarded` so the id is never issued again, and takes no other flag; answers already recorded from it stay in the ledger, and `--json` prints `{ discarded, path, next }`. |
| `readback --targets <ref>,... [--tolerance <t>] [--results <file>] [--plan <plan_id>]` | Renders a plain-English readback of each approve target (`source.model`, `source.model.field`, `metric:<name>`, `access`, `schema-pair:<source>:<from>:<to>` or `golden:<name>`) and writes it to `.semlayer/pending/<readback_id>.json` (see .semlayer/pending), never over an existing file. Prints the numbered items, the readback id and, per item, the `semlayer approve` (for a golden, `semlayer goldens confirm`, with the readback's `--results` or `--plan`) to run only after the owner says yes; goldens whose source is an export source, which cannot be confirmed until it has a connection (decision D2), are named once per source, at the place of the first, as `semlayer review apply` names them: "golden:<g> cannot be confirmed until source <s> has a connection: semlayer setup --source <s> --dialect <d> --env <VAR>, then semlayer goldens confirm --name <g>" (several goldens joined with "and"; `--database <path>` for duckdb). An unknown target is an error naming it, before anything is written. So is a target `semlayer approve` would refuse after the owner's yes for what its file lacks (one check, shared with `semlayer approve`): a model without `grain` or `primary_key`, a measure or metric without a description, a metric whose numerator, denominator or `{model.measure}` placeholders are not measure references or name a measure that does not exist (a metric's draft measures are left to `semlayer approve`, since the owner may approve them first), and anything else `semlayer validate` would raise of the items once approved that it does not raise now, such as a `currency` missing on a measure or metric with format `currency` or a to-one join whose evidence shows fan-out; approving never adds a validate issue. That check leaves out the approval's own bookkeeping (its hash and the owner's yes) and a join without evidence (`semlayer approve` warns of it; decision D2). It covers who sees the item: an untagged field that looks sensitive is refused with the edit `semlayer tag <ref> <pii, sensitive or public, as the owner said>`, and a dimension the default audience would see through a join to a model it does not see is refused with the fix its words name. The error names each item and what it lacks, as `once approved, semlayer validate says <file> <path>: <message>` for the general check, and gives the one `semlayer apply --file -` whose edits supply them, with a `<placeholder>` for each value (`currency`: `<ISO 4217 code, such as USD, as the owner said>`; a fan-out join's `fanout_override`: `<why the owner accepts the data, in their words>`), and `next` `semlayer apply --file -`; a lack no single `set` gives is fixed as its words say. A golden's rows show the columns of currency measures and metrics to two decimals, as `semlayer goldens run` and `confirm` print them; each figure two decimals do not show in full is listed with its full figure, which `semlayer goldens confirm` records and later runs compare (the hash covers the full figures). A `golden:<name>` target compiles the golden's request from the current YAML and runs it with its `cross_check_sql`, live or from `--results <file>` or `--plan <plan_id>` as `semlayer goldens run` does, and reads back its rows with the tolerance `--tolerance <t>` gives (else the golden's recorded tolerance, or 0.005); `--tolerance`, `--results` and `--plan` are refused without a golden target. For a golden on a source the CLI cannot query, without `--results` or `--plan`, it writes nothing and names the steps: `semlayer goldens run --name <g> --emit-sql`, `semlayer results add`, then `semlayer readback --targets golden:<g> --plan <plan_id>`. `--json`: `{ readback_id, path, items, next, next_steps, batched?, protected_note? }`, `path` relative to the semantic root, `next` the `semlayer reply --readback <readback_id> --item <n> ...` that records the owner's answer and `next_steps` the steps above, in the items' order. A readback of more than one item, none waiting on an export source's upgrade, is one owner turn: `batched` is `{ reply, reply_approve, approve, confirm_each }`, `semlayer reply --readback <id> --answers -`, `semlayer reply --readback <id> --answers - --approve`, which records the answers and approves every yes in one command, and the one `semlayer approve --readback <id>` (and `semlayer goldens confirm --readback <id>` for goldens) that it runs, and a line of the plain output names `reply_approve` in place of the steps item by item. `semlayer goldens confirm --readback` takes no `--results` or `--plan`, so for a readback made with either the goldens stay in `confirm_each` as their `semlayer goldens confirm --name <g>` steps with it (empty otherwise), and each such golden's item stores the flag as `rows_from`. When an item shows a column as protected, `protected_note` (and a plain line) says how a column the owner says is not personal or sensitive data comes out of protection: its field tagged `public` in place of `pii` or `sensitive` in the turn's `semlayer apply`, then `semlayer profile --source <s> --tables <table> --refresh`. |
| `remove-attribute <name>` | Removes a user attribute. Refuses, naming each with the command that changes it, while a role's `assign_when`, a restriction's `allow.when`, a row filter's `expr` or `hierarchy.user`, or a golden's `user` reads it. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, warnings, name, attribute }`. |
| `remove-cross-schema --source <s> --from <schema> --to <schema> [--keys <table>.<column>=<table>.<column>[,...]]` | Removes a schema pair from the source's `cross_schema`, a declined one included, or with `--keys` only those keys, each one the pair lists, which sends the pair back to draft; a pair left with no key is removed, and the last pair takes the `cross_schema` key with it. Refuses a pair the source does not declare in that direction (naming the other direction when it does) and a key the pair does not list. See Schema scope, Schema pairs. `--json`: `{ changed, file, removed_pair, removed_keys, pair, next }`. |
| `remove-field <source.model.field> [--keep-column] [--force]` | Removes a dimension, measure or join, and adds each column of the model's table it read (for a join, its key columns) that nothing else reads, by the rules `ignore-column` checks, to `ignored_columns` at its sorted place, unless `--keep-column`. Refuses an approved item unless `--force`, given only after the owner's yes; it then says the model changed since its approval and is to be read back (a model's approval hash covers its table, grain and keys, not its fields, so it stays approved; `semlayer approve --target <source.model> --revoke` withdraws it). Refuses, listing each, while anything names the item: a measure reference in `metrics.yaml`, a golden's request, an `access.yaml` restriction target or `deny.fields` entry written out in full, or a join reference in a model's expr or filter or in a row filter. A missing item is an error. When the snapshot holds sample values of a column it adds, it names `semlayer profile --source <s> --redact`. Removing a model's last join removes `joins`; its last dimension or measure leaves `[]`. Names the model's open questions that start `<field>: `, which it leaves as they are. See Typed edits. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, kind, ignored_columns_added, ignored_columns, redact, was_approved }`. |
| `remove-join --source <s> --model <m> --name <join> [--keep-column] [--force]` | Removes a join as `remove-field <s>.<m>.<join>` does (an approved one only with `--force`, after the owner's yes, the refusal naming `remove-join ... --force`; its key columns nothing else reads go to `ignored_columns` unless `--keep-column`). Refuses, naming each, while a dimension or measure (as `source.model.field`) or a row filter reads through it; a name that is a dimension or measure is refused, naming `remove-field`. See Typed edits. `--json`: as `remove-field`. |
| `remove-metric <name \| metric:<name>> [--force]` | Removes a metric from `metrics.yaml`; the last one leaves `metrics: []`. Refuses, listing each, while anything names it: a golden's request in its source or an `access.yaml` restriction target written out in full (a metric names measures only, never another metric). Refuses an approved metric unless `--force`, given only after the owner's yes. A missing metric is an error. See Typed edits. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, was_approved }`. |
| `remove-restriction <name>` | Removes a restriction; the last one takes the `restrictions` key with it. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, warnings, name, restriction }`. |
| `remove-role <name>` | Removes a role. Refuses, naming each with the command that changes it, while `default_role`, a restriction's `allow.roles` or a golden's `role` names it. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, warnings, name, role }`. |
| `remove-row-filter --role <r> --model <source.model> [--index <n>]` | Removes a role's row filter on a model; when the role has several there it lists them, numbered, and takes `--index`. The last one takes the `row_filters` key with it. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, warnings, filter, role }`. |
| `remove-segment --source <s> --model <m> --name <segment>` | Removes a segment from the model; the last one takes the `segments` key with it. Refuses, naming each and the `set` or `goldens new` that takes the segment out, while a measure's `segments` or a golden's request `segments` names it (`segment_in_use`), and a segment the model does not declare (`segment_missing`); nothing is written. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, next }`. See Typed edits. |
| `rename <source.model \| source.model.field \| metric:<name>> <new_name>` | Renames a model, dimension, measure, join or metric and every reference to it, in one write. A model (see Renaming a model) moves `models/<source>/<model>.yaml` to `<new_name>.yaml`; for a field or metric it rewrites measure references in `metrics.yaml` (`numerator`, `denominator` and `{model.measure}` in `expr`), the requests of goldens in its source (`model.field`, keeping a `:grain`, or a metric's bare name), `access.yaml` restriction targets and `deny.fields` entries written out in full, and join references (`<join>.<column>`, `<join>.<join>.<column>`) in every model's exprs and filters and in row filters. Refuses a name the model, the source's models or `metrics.yaml` already has; `access`; and a rename that would change whether an `access.yaml` list (a restriction's `targets`, a role's `deny.fields`) covers the item, read as `semlayer access` reads it (a `metric:` target matched against the metric's bare name, globs and `re:` included), since a rename never changes who sees what; an entry with the new name written out in full in the same list keeps the coverage. Names the model's open questions that start `<field>: `, which it leaves as they are. The renamed item when it is approved, and every approved item a reference update changes, are listed in `reapprove`. The current name changes nothing. See Typed edits. `--json`: `{ changed, file, files, reapprove, ref, new_ref, references: [{ file, path, from, to }] }`, and for a model also `renamed_file: { from, to }` (`null` when nothing changed) and `goldens`, the confirmed goldens whose request it changed. |
| `reply --readback <readback_id> --answers <file \| -> [--approve]` | Records one owner turn's answers to a readback: the file, or stdin with `-`, holds `{"by": "<owner>", "answers": [{"item": 1, "verdict": "yes", "text": "<owner's words>"}, {"items": [2, 3], "verdict": "change", "text": "...", "about": "access"}]}`. Every entry is checked before any line is written (the readback exists, each item exists and is named once, a verdict of `yes`, `no` or `change`, words, `about: access` only on a change to an item other than `access`); on any error nothing is written and the message names the entry. Then one `kind: reply` line per item, in one write, each the line the single form writes for it; `items` gives each item its own line with the same words, for words the owner said of each of them. Warns as the single form does, and names `semlayer approve --readback <id>` (and `semlayer goldens confirm --readback <id>`) for the yeses and one `semlayer apply` then `semlayer readback` for the changes. `--item` takes one number; a list is refused, naming `--answers`. `--approve` then acts on the yeses in the same command: once every line is recorded, it approves each non-golden item of the readback this call answered, as `semlayer approve --readback <id>` does (the same code, kept to those items), and confirms each golden this call answered, as `semlayer goldens confirm --readback <id>` does; each rests on its item's own latest reply being a yes to it as it stands, with the same refusals, and the output gives their own report of what was approved or confirmed and every other item with why. A golden read back from `--results` or `--plan`, or one whose export source waits on an upgrade, is not confirmed there: it is listed with why and its own `semlayer goldens confirm --name <g> --results <file>` (or the upgrade), and does not fail the command. The answers stay recorded when an approval is refused (an item changed since it was read back); the steps that remain are named, and it exits 1 when an item answered yes was not approved or confirmed, as `semlayer approve --readback` does. `--approve` goes only with `--answers`; the MCP tool does not offer it. `--json` adds `approval` and `confirmation`, the `--json` output of the approve and confirm it ran, and `confirm_each`, `[{ name, item, reason, next }]` for each golden left to its own confirm; `next` and `next_steps` are the steps left after them. |
| `reply --readback <readback_id> --item <n> --text <text> [--verdict yes\|no\|change] [--about access] --by <owner>` | Appends the owner's answer to one item of a readback to `.semlayer/ledger.jsonl` as a `kind: reply` line (see .semlayer/ledger.jsonl): the item's target, the content hash the item stored at readback time, the owner's words with credentials redacted, the verdict (`null` without `--verdict`) and the time. Refuses a readback `.semlayer/pending/` does not hold or an item number it lacks, naming `semlayer readback`. Warns, and still records, when the target's content hash now differs from the stored one ("changed since it was read back; read it back again with semlayer readback --targets <t>") or the target no longer exists. A golden's rows are not rerun here, so a `golden:<name>` reply warns only when the golden file is gone. Names the next command: for `yes` on an unchanged target, `semlayer approve --target <t> --by "<owner>"` (for a golden, `semlayer goldens confirm --name <g>`; for a golden whose source is an export source, which cannot be confirmed until it has a connection, the upgrade first: `semlayer setup --source <s> --dialect <d> --env <VAR>` (`--database <path>` for duckdb), then `semlayer goldens confirm --name <g>`, the yes staying recorded); for `no` or `change`, a typed edit and then `semlayer readback --targets <t>` (and, for a target other than `access`, that a change only to who sees it is recorded with `--about access`); without a verdict, the `semlayer reply` that records one. `--about access` records a change to who sees the item, not to the item: the line carries `about: access` with `verdict: change` (the default with `--about`; another verdict is refused, as are any other `--about` value and an item that is `access.yaml` itself), it is not a reply to the item's content (see .semlayer/ledger.jsonl), and the next command it names is the `semlayer reply` that records the owner's answer to the item itself, or the item's `semlayer approve` when their yes to it already stands. `--json`: `{ id, path, entry, current_hash, hash_changed, redacted, warnings, next }`. |
| `results add --plan <plan_id> --query <query id> --file <rows.json>` | Stores one query's result for an emitted plan, refusing a result of exactly the query's `max_rows` rows, and names the next command: the next `semlayer results add`, or the plan's `finish` command (see Stored plans). `--json`: `{ plan_id, query, rows, path, state, remaining, complete, next }`. |
| `results status --plan <plan_id>` | Lists which queries of an emitted plan have results and names the next command: the next `semlayer results add`, or the plan's `finish` command (see Stored plans). `--json`: `{ plan_id, command, source, dialect, path, queries: [{ id, purpose, max_rows, stored, rows? }], remaining, complete, finish, next }`. |
| `review apply <file> --by <owner>` | Records a filled review form's answers in `.semlayer/ledger.jsonl` (see Applying a filled form): each answered item as `semlayer reply` would, refusing an item whose content hash changed since export, and each answered open question as `semlayer question resolve` would. Refuses an unknown readback and a malformed form, writing nothing; a second apply of the same form records nothing. Never approves; names `semlayer approve --target <t> --by "<owner>"` for each yes (for a golden, `semlayer goldens confirm --name <g>`; for goldens whose source is an export source, once per source, "golden:<g> cannot be confirmed until source <s> has a connection: semlayer setup --source <s> --dialect <d> --env <VAR>, then semlayer goldens confirm --name <g>" (several goldens joined with "and"; `--database <path>` for duckdb), the yes staying recorded). A golden's rows are not rerun here: its item counts as changed only when the golden file is gone, and `semlayer goldens confirm` rechecks the rows. `--json`: `{ readback_id, file, recorded, already_recorded, refused, questions_already_resolved, questions_not_found, unanswered: { items, questions }, edited, redacted, ledger_path, next, next_steps }`, `recorded` the appended ledger entries. |
| `review export --readback <readback_id> --format md\|json [--out <path>] [--force]` | Writes the pending readback as a form the owner fills in (see Review forms): per item its number, target, readback text and content hash, an empty verdict (yes, no or change) and an empty answer, then the open questions of the models and `access.yaml` the items belong to, each with an empty answer. Markdown for the owner, JSON for a tool. Writes `.semlayer/reviews/<readback_id>.<md\|json>` or `--out`, a path resolved from the semantic root that it refuses outside it, never over an existing file unless `--force`. Refuses an unknown readback and one whose items changed since it was read back (a `golden:<name>` item only when its file is gone, since its rows are not rerun here). Prints the path and the next step, `semlayer review apply <path> --by "<owner>"`. `--json`: `{ readback_id, format, path, item_count, question_count, next }`, `path` relative to the semantic root. |
| `run --source <s> (--sql <query> \| --file <path>) [--limit <n>] [--show-protected]` | Runs one read-only query against a duckdb or postgres source. Every row is counted and at most `--limit` rows, 100 by default, are shown. A result column whose name, or the field that reads it, is protected (`pii`, `sensitive`, `ignored_columns`, or flagged by `scripts/lib/sensitivity.ts`) shows its values as `[protected]`, and so does any column of a statement that reads a protected column unless it is a count or reads nothing protected (see Protected values in results); `--show-protected` shows them, and the skill never passes it. A result with two columns of one name is refused with a message to alias them. |
| `scope --source <s> [--include <p>]... [--exclude <p>]... [--remove <p>]... [--tables a,b] [--from-file <path>] [--to-file <path>] [--force] [--clear] [--write] [--explain <table>] [--reason <text>]` | Previews the tables a scope change selects, with counts by schema and each entry's match count (see Explain and match counts); `--write` saves it. `--tables` adds exact names. `--from-file` (a path from the working directory) checks a table-list file, previews the scope with its entries in place of the saved file's, and with `--write` copies it to `scope/<s>.txt` and sets `scope.tables_file`. `--to-file` (a path from the semantic root, refused outside it and on the files semlayer manages) writes the previewed selection as a table-list file of exact names; it refuses an empty selection, which would read back as every table. Neither replaces an existing file with other contents without `--force`. `--clear` also drops `scope.tables_file`. `--json` adds `tables_file` (`{ path, include, exclude }` entry counts, or null), `to_file`, `entries` and `by_schema`. `--explain <table>` says which entry puts the table in or out of the previewed scope, with the reason recorded for an exclude, and refuses `--write` and `--to-file`. `--reason <text>` records why this command's `--exclude` patterns leave tables out (see Explain and match counts). `--include`, `--exclude` and `--tables` add to the saved scope, whose entries stay, so leaving one more table out is `semlayer scope --source <s> --exclude <table> --write`; `--remove` takes an entry out and `--clear` starts over; the human preview says so and `--json` adds `kept_entries`, the saved entries kept. See Table-list files. |
| `set <ref> <key> (<value> \| --unset)` | Sets one key of a model, field, metric or `access` (`mode` and `default_role` at the top level; one key of a role, restriction or user attribute as `set access role:<name> \| restriction:<name> \| attribute:<name> <key> ...`, see Access edits), of a golden (`golden:<g>`: `role`, `user`, `reference` and `tolerance`) or of a source (`source:<s>`: `description`), or removes it with `--unset`; `<ref>` is otherwise written as for `approve`. With no `access.yaml`, `set access mode open\|closed` creates a draft one (see Typed edits) and every other `set access` is refused, naming it. A key whose schema is text or a choice of words takes the value as written (trimmed), quotes, colons and `#` included; any other key reads it as YAML, so `[a, b]` is a list; a list of names (`primary_key`, `tags`, `synonyms`, `time_grains`) also takes `a,b`, so `primary_key order_id,line_no` and `primary_key '[order_id, line_no]'` write the same list (one name alone is written `[a]`). A model has no `filter`: `set <source.model> filter <rule>` is refused with the one `semlayer apply` that writes the rule on each of the model's measures, joined with `and` to a filter the measure already has. Refuses the approval fields (`status`, `approved_by`, `approved_at`, `approved_hash`, `last_approved`), naming `semlayer approve`; keys the item's JSON Schema lacks, listing those it takes; and keys another command owns (`name`, `model`, `dimensions`, `measures`, `joins`, `ignored_columns`, `open_questions`, `evidence`), naming it. Setting `tags` is refused as `tag` refuses it. An `expr` or `filter` of a dimension or measure that newly reads a column in `ignored_columns`, on the model or on a model its join references reach, is refused, naming `ignore-column --remove`, and so are a join's `keys` or `to` that newly make a key column one listed in either model's `ignored_columns` (see Ignored columns in typed edits). Setting a key of an approved `access.yaml` makes the file a draft again in the same write (see Access edits). A new key goes before the item's `status` (after `mode` in `access.yaml`). See Typed edits. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, key, value }`, plus `created: true` when it created `access.yaml`, `returned_to_draft: true` and `last_approved` when it made an approved `access.yaml` a draft, and for an entry of `access.yaml` `warnings` and the entry's name under `role`, `restriction` or `attribute`. |
| `setup --print-role-sql --dialect <d> --schemas a,b [--role-name <name>] [--database <name>] [--warehouse <name>] [--catalog <name>] [--project <id>]` | Prints the statements a database administrator runs to create a least-privilege, read-only role limited to the schemas, and never connects or runs them (see Setup, Printing a read-only role). Needs no project and writes nothing. `--json`: `{ dialect, role, schemas, language, statements, text, placeholders, next }`. |
| `setup --source <s> --dialect <d> (--env VAR \| --env-prefix PREFIX \| --export <file> [--reason <text>]) [--schemas a,b] [--include <p>]... [--exclude <p>]... [--tables-file <path>] [--database <path>] [--view name=relation]... [--name <project>]` | Creates or updates a source in one step, connection first (see Setup): `init` when there is no project, the source file written or updated in place, the scope, the catalog read (`introspect`, or `import` of the export copied under the root), then the scope preview and one next action. Refuses without `--env`, `--env-prefix` or `--export` (a duckdb source's `--database` or `--view` is its connection), refuses `--env` with `--env-prefix`, `--env-prefix` on a source that is not postgres, and in `--env` anything that looks like a URL or a credential, as `add-source` does. Adds the env-file lines to the `.gitignore` beside the root, as `init` does, and, when the connection variables are set nowhere, writes `.env.example` beside the root's `.env` with their placeholder lines, or appends the lines an existing one lacks (see Connection variables and env files). `--json`: `{ project_created, project, source: { name, path, created, updated, changes }, intake: { mode, schemas, reason?, export_file? }, snapshot: { read, current, tables?, columns?, changes }, scope, changed, warnings, next, upgraded, changes, profiled, joins_checked, joins_skipped, contradictions }`, `scope` being null without a snapshot; the last six describe the upgrade of an export source to a connection (see Setup, Upgrade from an export) and are `false`, `null` and empty lists otherwise; on a postgres connection it adds `role_report` (see Setup, The role report) and `connection_from` (see Connection variables and env files). |
| `setup --source <s> --dialect <d> (--env VAR \| --env-prefix PREFIX \| --export <file>) --primary <schema>` | Names the source's primary schema in `intake.primary` (see Schema scope), with every other flag and step of the form above; refuses a name that is not a schema name and one missing from a non-empty `intake.schemas`. Prints the primary schema. `--json`: as the form above, with `intake.primary` (the primary schema, set or derived, else null) and `source.changes.primary` (true when `intake.primary` changed). |
| `setup --source <s> --dialect <d> (--env VAR \| --env-prefix PREFIX \| --export <file>) --week-start <day>` | Sets the day the source's weeks start in `calendar.week_start` (see Metric semantics, Calendar and default time dimension), one of `monday` to `sunday`, with every other flag and step of the form above; refuses any other value. Changing the day changes the approval hash of each dimension of the source with a week grain, and setup says so. `--json`: as the form above, with `source.changes.week_start` (true when `calendar.week_start` changed). |
| `sql --source <s> --request <file\|json> [--role <r>] [--user <json object of attributes>]` | Compiles a request to SQL, with its joins and bound parameters, for a postgres or duckdb source, as the user `--role` and `--user` describe, and never runs it (see Reference compiler); exits 1 with the reason when it refuses one. `--request` is a JSON object or a YAML or JSON file holding a request, or a golden file, whose `role` and `user` apply unless the flags are given. `--source` may be left out when the request names its source. |
| `status [--source <s>]` | Shows progress per source, the next batch of tables to interview and the next step. Lists the approved items that changed since their approval, and per source the columns without a field (`new_columns`), the tables new in scope (`new_in_scope`) and the approved models whose tables left scope (`left_scope_with_models`). For an export source it lists each data-dependent step as waiting on a connection and ends with the `semlayer setup` that adds one (see semlayer status --json). |
| `tables --source <s> [--groups] [--all]` | Lists in-scope tables, or all with `--all`, with size, modeling and profiling state; `--groups` clusters them by schema, prefix and suffix and suggests patterns. A table is profiled (`yes`) when its `profiled_at` is set, `partial` when some columns have profiles but `profiled_at` is missing (a reintrospect added or retyped columns), and otherwise `no`; `--json` carries it as `profile_state`. When the source has usage evidence (see Usage evidence), each row also carries `usage`, the statements of the saved queries that read the table (a `used` column in plain output), `--json` adds `unused` (the in-scope tables listed that no saved query reads), and plain output names them; without it the output is unchanged. |
| `tag <ref> <tag>[,<tag>...] [--remove]` | Adds tags to a model, dimension, measure or metric, or removes them with `--remove`, keeping the list's order; a first tag writes a flow list after the item's `synonyms` or `description`, and removing the last tag removes the key. Adding `pii` or `sensitive` names `semlayer profile --source <s> --redact`; removing one says everyone who may see the item sees its values once approved. On an approved item, removing `pii` or `sensitive` or adding `public` is refused, naming `semlayer approve --target <ref> --revoke`, unless it restores the content the item's `approved_hash` records. See Typed edits. `--json`: `{ changed, file, reapprove, ref, added, tags }`, with `removed` in place of `added` under `--remove`. |
| `usage add --source <s> <path>... [--schema <name>]... [--query-key <name>]` | Reads saved queries, scratch files and query logs as evidence of what people query, connecting to nothing and running none of it (see Usage evidence), and adds what they say to `snapshots/<s>.usage.json`. `--json`: `{ source, file, added, already_read, others_not_read, summary, next }`. |
| `usage clear --source <s>` | Removes `snapshots/<s>.usage.json`, the usage evidence `usage add` wrote. |
| `usage show --source <s> [--table <t>]` | Prints the usage evidence of `snapshots/<s>.usage.json` per table with the sentences to put to the owner (`--json`: the file's content for the tables shown, each with `evidence`). |
| `validate [--codes]` | Checks structure and references, and errors on a credential in a duckdb source's `connection.database`, `connection.setup` or `connection.views`, naming the key, never the value, and on a setup statement outside the allowlist or a view that is not one table expression (see sources); errors on a source with both `connection.env` and `connection.env_prefix`, on an env file under the semantic root and on an env file the connection lookup reads that git tracks (see Connection variables and env files); exits 1 on any error. Errors on an approved item that "changed since it was approved" and warns once per file on approved items without a hash while no item records one, and on each approved item without a hash once others record one (see Approval hashes); errors on an approved to-one join whose evidence shows fan-out without `fanout_override`, on a golden request whose measures come from more than one model and are not one metric, and on a `time_zone` that is not an IANA zone or not on a time dimension; warns on a golden whose `cross_check_sql` never names a column a requested measure's rule reads, on a golden that writes it under its older name `sql`, and errors on one that has both (see goldens). Warns when an approved field the default audience sees looks sensitive and carries no `pii`, `sensitive` or `public` tag: "<field> is visible to everyone and looks sensitive (<reason>); tag it pii or sensitive, or tag it public if everyone may see it", where a reason found on a column the field reads starts "it reads column <c>; ". Checks every golden's request against access as the user it runs as, warns on a role's row-filtered model whose child models it does not limit (see Row filters), on text that quotes a raw value of a pii or sensitive column (see goldens), and on a snapshot holding raw or hashed sample values of a protected column (see Profiles of protected columns). Warns, `model.primary_key_not_unique`, when stored data shows a declared `primary_key` is not unique, with no query: a one-column key whose profile covers the whole table (`sampled: false`, an exact-count dialect) counts fewer distinct values than non-null rows, or a bridge model whose key is exactly the two key columns of a bridge join through it whose current `evidence.through` counts repeated pairs; it says what was measured and that rows cannot be told apart by the key, and names `semlayer set <source>.<model> primary_key` or a grain that says rows repeat.
