The model's readback states the same fact beside its key. `--json`: `{ ok, errors, warnings, issues }`, each issue `{ level, file, path, message, code, next? }` (see Codes and errors). `--codes` prints every registered issue code with its level and meaning, grouped by family, reading no layer; with `--json` an array of `{ code, level, meaning, next? }`. |

### Errors and malformed files

A command that refuses exits 1 with one plain line on stderr, `semlayer <command>: <message>`, which names the next command where there is one. Exit code 2 is an error in the tool itself, printed with its stack trace.

The loaders the commands share for `semantic.yaml`, a source, a snapshot, a model, `metrics.yaml`, `access.yaml` and a golden check a file's shape as they read it, against the JSON Schema `semlayer validate` uses for that kind of file (`schemas/<kind>.schema.json`). The check follows the schema through the whole file and compares shapes: a map, a list, or a single value (text, a number, true or false). A part that holds one where the schema names another is refused in one line, and so is a file that is empty, cannot be read or does not parse:

```
<file, from the semantic root>: <place> should be <expected> (found <kind>). Fix the file, or run semlayer validate for the full list.
<file, from the semantic root>: is not valid YAML (<the parser's first line>). Fix the file, or run semlayer validate for the full list.
```

`<place>` is a key (`metrics`, `measures[0].name`) or `the file`, and a kind is `a map`, `a list`, `text`, `a number`, `true or false`, `nothing` or `an empty file`: `metrics.yaml: metrics should be a list (found a number).` The second line reads `is not valid JSON (...)` for a snapshot, `is a directory, not a file.` or `cannot be read (<code>).` where that is the trouble. The parser's later lines, which quote the file, are not printed.

The check stops there, so that a half-written layer still loads:

- A single value of another kind (a number where text belongs), a missing key, a key the schema does not know and a value outside its list all load. The commands read around them and `semlayer validate` lists each. A key with no value reads as a key that is not there.
- What must be there is a model's `table` and, in a snapshot, which only `semlayer` writes, every key its schema requires.
- A `status` and a `scope.tables_file` load whatever they hold, and so does an item of `metrics` with no value, because the commands read each in words of their own: a status that is not `approved` is a draft, `semlayer scope` reports and clears a `tables_file` that is not a path, and an empty metric is skipped.
- A `metrics.yaml` or `access.yaml` that holds nothing but comments loads as nothing written yet, and a golden file like it is passed over where goldens are listed, though `semlayer validate` lists the empty file. Any other empty file is refused, an empty golden asked for by name among them.
- `access.yaml` is checked no further than being a map, because the access evaluator reads each of its parts closed (see access.yaml) and `semlayer status` names a part it cannot read.

The check reads a 50,000-table, 1,000,000-column snapshot in about 0.1 s, beside the 0.13 s its JSON takes to parse.

`semlayer validate` reads every file itself and lists each problem, a malformed file's among them, and does not stop at the first.

With `--json`, every refusal and every error in the tool itself also prints one JSON object on stdout beside the line on stderr, `{ "error", "code", "next"? }` (see Codes and errors), so `semlayer status --json` and `semlayer next --json` in an empty directory print `code` `no_layer` with `next` `semlayer init --name <name>`. A malformed layer file, pending readback or ledger carries `next` `semlayer validate`.

### Codes and errors

**Issues.** `semlayer validate --json` prints `{ "ok", "errors", "warnings", "issues" }`, and each issue is `{ "level", "file", "path", "message", "code", "next"? }` (`schemas/issue.schema.json`). `code` is stable across releases, so an agent branches on it rather than on the message, which may be reworded; `next`, where present, is the command that fixes or explains the issue. The human lines of `semlayer validate` do not show codes. `semlayer apply --json` carries the warnings its entries add as `warning_issues`, each `{ "code", "file", "path", "message" }`, beside the unchanged `warnings` strings.

**Codes.** A code is a family and a name, `family.name` in lowercase letters, digits and underscores, such as `schema.required`; the prefix is the family, whatever the file that registers it is called. Each family is registered in a file under `scripts/lib/issue-codes/` that maps each code to its usual level, a one-line meaning and, where one exists, `next`; the registry reads every file there, so a new family is a new file. `semlayer validate --codes` prints every registered code, grouped by family and sorted, with its level and meaning; with `--json` it prints an array of `{ "code", "level", "meaning", "next"? }`. It reads no layer and needs no `--root`. This section carries no table of codes, so the command is the list. A code is added or retired, never renamed or reused, and its level is the registered level.

**Families.**
- `schema.*`: the JSON Schema keyword that failed (`schema.required`, `schema.unknown_property`, `schema.enum`, `schema.type`, `schema.pattern`, and `schema.other` for the rest).
- `source.*`, `intake.*` and `env.*`: a source's connection, scope and snapshot, its intake block, and env files. `source.connection_missing`, `source.pooler_startup_parameter` and `source.ssl_root_cert_unreadable` come from refusals (see below), not from `semlayer validate`.
- `scope.*`: declared schema pairs, their keys, and the primary schema.
- `file.*`, `ledger.*`, `ref.*`, `expr.*`, `model.*`, `field.*`, `time.*`, `ignore.*`, `approval.*`, `access.*` and `golden.*`: the checks of a layer's own files, a file's name, place and parse, the ledger, a name that resolves to nothing or is taken, SQL text and join references, a model's table and joins, fields, time dimensions and zones, `ignored_columns`, approvals and their hashes, access, and goldens. The model, field, join or filter an issue is about is in its path, not in its code.
- `metric.*`: segments, the default time dimension, semi-additive measures and a golden's trailing window, and the refusals `semlayer sql` gives a request that misuses them (`metric.segments_on_metric`, `metric.default_time_missing`, `metric.semi_additive_mixed`).
- `join.*`: bridge joins, their dedupe rule and their evidence, and the refusals `semlayer sql` gives a multi-fact request that names segments or a request grouped through a bridge it cannot share a figure over.
- `release.*`: retyped columns in the drift record, `changelog.yaml` and `layer_version`, and the refusals of `semlayer change start` and `semlayer change close`.

`next` is set on `approval.hash_changed` (`semlayer readback --targets <ref>`), `approval.no_ledger_yes` (the `semlayer readback --targets` the message names) and `approval.stamp_hashes` (`semlayer approve --stamp-hashes`). The warnings `time.zone_carries_own`, `expr.divides_by_literal` and `ignore.column_read` can never be right, so a typed edit that adds one is refused (see Typed edits). A rule file under `scripts/lib/validate-rules/` passes a registered code as the last argument of every `error` and `warn` call.

**The error envelope.** With `--json`, a command that fails prints one object on stdout, `{ "error": "<message>", "code": "<code>", "next": "<command>"? }` (`schemas/cli-error.schema.json`), beside its unchanged line on stderr and with its exit code unchanged; a command that has already printed its own JSON, such as `semlayer validate` with errors, prints no second object. A code of the form `family.name` is registered in `scripts/lib/issue-codes/`: the refusal is the refusal of that issue (`approval.no_ledger_yes`, when `semlayer approve` finds no matching owner yes in the ledger; `source.connection_missing`, when `--env-file` or `SEMLAYER_ENV_FILE` names no file; `source.pooler_startup_parameter`, when a connection pooler refuses a startup parameter; `source.ssl_root_cert_unreadable`, when the CA certificate file named for TLS cannot be read; and, on a typed edit refused for a value that can never be right, the code of the warning it names; `semlayer apply` passes on the code of its one refused entry, or of all of them when they share one). A bare word is a refusal that is no validate issue:

- `user_error`: any refusal without a code of its own;
- `usage`: a flag the command does not take, a missing flag value or an unknown command (`next` is `semlayer <command> --help` or `semlayer help`);
- `internal`: an error in the tool itself, exit 2, with no `next`;
- `semantic_needs_dbt_manifest`: `semlayer import --semantic` on a file that is not a dbt manifest (`next` is the `semlayer import --source <name> --file <manifest.json> --semantic` form);
- `no_layer`: no semantic layer at the root (`next` is `semlayer init --name <name>`);
- `bad_target`: a target that is not `source.model`, `source.model.field`, `metric:<name>`, `golden:<name>`, `access` or `schema-pair:<source>:<from>:<to>`;
- `by_mismatch`: `semlayer approve --by` names someone other than the owner whose yes is recorded;
- `readback_blocked`: `semlayer readback` of items `semlayer approve` would refuse after the owner's yes (`next` is `semlayer apply --file -`, or, when no typed edit gives what is missing and one command does, that command, such as `semlayer check-join` for a bridge join without bridge evidence);
- `batch_refused`: `semlayer apply` refused several entries whose codes differ (each entry's own message is in the error);
- `owner_words_missing`: `semlayer add-metric --none` without the owner's name (`--by`) or words (`--text`) (`next` is the `semlayer add-metric --none` form);
- `input_unreadable` and `input_invalid`: an `--answers` file or stdin that cannot be read, or is not one JSON object.

### semlayer status --json

The skill resumes from this output.

- `settings`: `focus_threshold` and `batch_size`.
- `sources[]`: per source, its dialect, snapshot state, `needs_focus`, `unprofiled_tables` (the in-scope tables that are not profiled by the rule `semlayer tables` uses: `profiled_at` is not set) and `partially_profiled_tables` (those of them some of whose columns carry a profile), model, dimension, measure and join counts by status, joins without evidence and open questions, `draft_models` (the draft models on in-scope tables, which alone hold the interview phase open), `draft_fields` (the draft dimensions, measures and joins of in-scope models, as `model.field`), `models_with_draft_fields` (approved models that still hold draft fields; the handoff names them), `new_columns` (`[{ model, column, type }]`: columns of the tables under in-scope approved or draft models that no field, join key or primary key of any model over the same table reads and no such model lists in `ignored_columns`; the human output words them "columns without a field") and `missing_columns` (`[{ model, field, column }]`: columns fields read that the snapshot no longer has), `new_in_scope` (tables the last `semlayer introspect` or `semlayer import` found newly in the selection that are still in it and have no model; the human output words them "new in scope, with no model yet") and `left_scope_with_models` (`[{ model, table }]`: approved models on tables a rerun found had left the selection and that are still out of it; the human output words them "left scope with approved models"). See Scope changes.
- `draft_metrics`: the names of the metrics in `metrics.yaml` that are not approved, one per metric `metrics.draft` counts; an entry without a name goes by its place in the list, as `metrics[1]`.
- `no_metrics`: `{ by, text, at, applies }`, the owner's answer that they report no metrics for now (see metrics.yaml, No metrics for now), or `null`; `applies` is false once `metrics.yaml` lists a metric. The human output shows it in one line, "metrics: none for now, said by <by> on <at>", or that it no longer applies, and while it applies the `done` message names it for the handoff.
- `pending_readback_items`: the items of the pending readbacks that wait for the owner's answer, as `semlayer next` asks them (see .semlayer/pending/<readback_id>.json).
- `access`: `{ state, roles, restrictions, open_questions, mode, default_audience, last_approved? }`: `missing`, `draft` or `approved`, the number of roles, the number of restrictions, the open questions of `access.yaml`, top level and per role, the mode (`open` without `access.yaml`), and what the default audience sees, as `{ models, fields, metrics, hidden_by_protected_tags }`: the visible counts and the number of approved items a protected tag hides from it. The human output names the mode and the default audience in one line. `last_approved` (`{ by, at }`) is present while the draft records the approval `semlayer approve --revoke` set aside, and the access message then says what `semlayer access` says: the version approved by <by> on <at> keeps applying where it is deployed, and tools that load the draft serve nothing.
- `open_questions`: the open questions across the reported sources' models and `access.yaml`.
- `changed_since_approval`: the approved items of the reported sources (models, their dimensions, measures and joins, and metrics) and `access.yaml` whose content no longer matches their `approved_hash`, as approve targets (`source.model.field`, `metric:<name>`, `access`). The human output names them in one line, "changed since approval: ...". An approved item without a hash is not listed here.
- `hash_missing`: the approved items of the same scope without `approved_hash` while another of them, or any metric, records one, as approve targets; their hash may have been removed. The human output names them in one line, "approved without approved_hash while others carry one: ...". Empty while no item records a hash.
- `approved_without_yes`: the approved items of the same scope, and the approved goldens (`golden:<name>`), whose owner's latest reply in `.semlayer/ledger.jsonl` is not a yes with the content hash they now have (approved before `semlayer approve` required one), as approve targets; an item in `changed_since_approval` or `hash_missing` is not listed. They stay approved and hold no phase open; the human output names them in one line, "approved without a recorded owner yes (they stay approved): ...".
- `next_batch`: `{ source, tables: [{ table, model, model_status, score, reasons }] }`, the next tables to interview, or `null`.
- `draft_goldens`: the names of the goldens that are not approved, one per golden `goldens.draft` counts (the name in the file, or its file name); an empty golden file is neither counted nor listed.
- `roles_without_goldens`: the roles in `access.yaml` that no approved golden runs as; a golden with no `role` runs as `default_role`. Any such role holds `next_step` in `verify` until a golden that runs as it is written and confirmed.
- `row_filter_joins_without_goldens`: `[{ role, model, filtered_model, join }]`, for each role, each model visible to it that reaches one of the role's row-filtered models through a to-one join of one or two hops (`model` and `filtered_model` as `source.model`; `join` the chain of join names from `model`, as a join reference writes it: `order`, or `order.customer`), when no approved golden running as that role names a field of that model. The human output names them in one line, and the handoff names each one.
- `next_step`: `{ phase, message, command, action, source?, after_owner_yes, blocked_on?, connection_upgrade? }`, the first unfinished phase. `action` is the step as data, `{ command, args, kind }`: the semlayer command, its arguments, and `kind` `run`, `ask_owner` (the owner is asked first, and `args` may hold a `<placeholder>` for their answer: no source yet, no metrics yet, no `access.yaml` (with goldens that name roles or user attributes, `semlayer apply --file -`, whose standard input `message` gives: `semlayer set access mode open|closed`, then a `semlayer add-attribute` for each user attribute the goldens give, typed from their values, and a `semlayer add-role` for each role they name, since `semlayer set access mode` alone is refused until those exist), a closed one with no roles, no draft golden left to read back, a role without a golden, the `connect` upgrade, a profile the database refused) or `done`; `command` is the action printed, `semlayer <command> <args>`, an argument other than a name, flag or placeholder in double quotes. `after_owner_yes` is true when `command` is a `semlayer approve` or `semlayer goldens confirm`, which runs only after the owner says yes to its readback. Phases in order: `add-source`, `introspect`, `drift`, `reapprove`, `scope`, `profile`, `draft`, `interview`, `check-join`, `metrics`, `access`, `verify`, `connect`, `done`. `drift` holds while an approved field reads a column the snapshot no longer has: the table changed, so the affected items are read back to the owner. `reapprove` holds while `changed_since_approval` or `hash_missing` lists an item: its message names them and asks for each to be read back as it now stands and approved again on the owner's yes, or restored, and its command signs them off (below). `scope` also holds, right after `reapprove`, while `left_scope_with_models` lists a model: its message names each model and table and says to keep it by bringing the table back into scope (its pattern, or its schema in `intake.schemas`) or to revoke it (`semlayer approve --target <s>.<model> --revoke`) or remove its model file, and its command previews them with `semlayer scope --source <s> --tables <tables>`, the tables named by the rule in Scope changes (a table gone from the snapshot opens `drift` first). Tables new in scope route to `draft` before other tables without a model, with the command `semlayer draft --source <s> --tables <them>`, the tables named by the same rule. New columns route to `draft`, with the command `semlayer draft --source <s> --new-columns`; a column the owner left out on purpose goes in the model's `ignored_columns` instead. `access` holds until `access.yaml` exists and is approved: without it, `next_step` asks the owner the exceptions question ("is there anything some people should not see?"); while `access.yaml` has open questions, `next_step` is `{ phase: access, blocked_on: "open_questions", command: "semlayer access", after_owner_yes: false }` and its message says the step waits on the owner's answers and names the first question; an approved `access.yaml` in open mode with no roles is complete. `verify` holds until there are 5 approved goldens and every role has one: its message counts them as `<approved> of <all> golden(s) approved; the verify phase needs 5 approved` (the human output: `goldens <approved>/<all> approved (the verify phase needs 5)`); with a draft golden its command reads the first back (`semlayer readback --targets golden:<g>`, or `semlayer goldens run --name <g> --emit-sql` on a source semlayer cannot run), else it asks the owner for a question with a known answer and names `semlayer goldens new`. The `profile` step names `semlayer profile --source <s>` while an in-scope table was never tried; once only tables the last run skipped are left (`profile_skipped`, per source `[{ table, reason }]`), it names a retry of those with `--tables` and what to change, or, when every one was refused for a missing SELECT permission, asks the owner for a grant or for `semlayer scope --exclude <them> --reason "<owner's reason>" --write`. `blocked_on` is `open_questions` while `access.yaml` or any model holds open questions, whatever the step (the step can be taken meanwhile). `done` means the phases the CLI can measure are complete; it is reachable with draft metrics, goldens and fields the owner deferred, and its message names each of them and ends by asking for the coverage checklist of the handoff card (`references/phases/handoff.md`) to be ticked before the handoff. A step that signs items off (`reapprove`: every item it lists; `interview`: the batch's first draft model's draft dimensions, measures and joins, then the model; `metrics`: the draft metrics; `access`) names `semlayer readback --targets <refs>`, with `after_owner_yes` false, until the owner's latest reply in the ledger is a yes to one of them as it now stands; it then names `semlayer approve --target <that ref>`, with `after_owner_yes` true.
- Export sources (decision D2). A source with `intake.mode: export` has `intake_mode: "export"`, `runnable: false`, and `waiting_on_connection`: `[{ step, message }]`, one entry for each data-dependent step, `profiles`, `join_evidence`, `goldens` and `drift_from_data`, which the human output lists under "waiting on a connection" (empty for a connection source). Its tables are not profiled, its joins skip `check-join` (the interview command goes straight to the sign-off readback), and with no snapshot the step is `semlayer import --source <s>`. `approved_joins_without_evidence` (`model.join`, every source) lists the approved joins with no evidence; the human output warns on them, and `semlayer validate` warns on each. While every reported source is an export source, `connect` replaces `verify`, since goldens need data; with any export source, `connect` replaces `done`, so status never reports done while a step waits on a connection. `connect`'s message names the waiting steps and `semlayer setup --print-role-sql`, and its command is the upgrade, `semlayer setup --source <s> --dialect <d> --env <VAR>` (for duckdb, `--database <path>`; see Setup, Upgrade from an export), which `semlayer next` runs at `connect` rather than reporting `done`. `next_step.connection_upgrade` holds that command whenever a reported source is an export source, and the human output ends with it.

### semlayer next --json

`semlayer next` reads `semlayer status`, `.semlayer/ledger.jsonl`, `.semlayer/pending/` and `.semlayer/plans/`, writes nothing, and prints exactly one action:

```json
{ "kind": "run", "phase": "interview", "why": "...", "action": { "command": "approve", "args": ["--target", "warehouse.orders.revenue"], "kind": "run" }, "run": { "command": "semlayer approve --target warehouse.orders.revenue" }, "blocked_on": null }
```

- `action`: `{ command, args, kind }`, the action as data; `run.command` and the plain output's command are it printed, and `semlayer next` reads `semlayer status`'s `next_step.action`, never a command's text. Unless `kind` is `ask_owner` or `dispatch`, its arguments hold no `<placeholder>` and name targets that exist.
- `kind`: `run` (`run.command` is the command), `ask_owner` (`ask_owner: { readback_id, text, items: [{ n, target, kind }] }`: read `text`, the open items of that readback numbered as stored, to the owner, then record each answer with `semlayer reply` (with more than one open item, `action` is `semlayer reply --readback <id> --answers - --approve` and `action.stdin` holds one answer per open item, `{"by": "<owner>", "answers": [{"item": <n>, "verdict": "yes|no|change", "text": "<owner's words>"}, ...]}`, each placeholder filled from the owner's answer); or, without `readback_id`, `ask_owner: { text, items: [] }`: ask the owner `text`, then run `action` with their answer in place of each placeholder, with `action.stdin`, when set, on its standard input; the plain output's second line is `Ask the owner, then: <command>`), `dispatch` (`dispatch: { role: "connector_runner", task: { plan_id, source, dialect, queries: [{ id, purpose, sql, max_rows }], store, finish } }`: the plan's queries without a stored result, the `semlayer results add` that stores each, and the command that finishes the plan), or `done`. `record_reply` is reserved: an answer the owner gave in chat is the agent's to record with `semlayer reply`, and no file says it was given, so `semlayer next` does not return it.
- `phase`: the `semlayer status` phase the action belongs to (`done` for reading back approved items without a recorded yes); `why`: the reason in plain words, naming the command; `blocked_on`: `open_questions` when `semlayer status`'s step waits on them, else `null`, whatever the action (the action can be taken meanwhile); or `connection: <variables> in <env file>` (below).
- `connection`, only while blocked on one: `{ source, variables, add_to, line_to_add }`. A `run` action that connects to a source (`introspect`, `profile`, `check-join`, `goldens run|confirm|check`, or a readback of a golden, without `--emit-sql`, `--plan` or `--results`) whose connection variables nothing sets (see Connection variables and env files) becomes `ask_owner`: `ask_owner.text` asks the owner to add `line_to_add` (placeholders for every value) to `add_to`, and `action` is `semlayer setup --source <s> --dialect <d> --env <VAR>` (or `--env-prefix <PREFIX>`), which checks the connection once it is set. No value is read into the output.
- The ledger is read as `semlayer approve` reads it: when `semlayer approve` would refuse it (a line that is not JSON, a reply line without the fields an approval needs, a cut-off last line, a ledger behind a link), no yes in it counts in `semlayer status`, `semlayer next`, `semlayer validate` or the typed edits, and `semlayer next` names `semlayer validate` with the reason before anything else.
- `access_changes`, only on an action of phase `access` while any is open: `[{ id, target, text, by }]`, the owner's changes to who sees an item (ledger lines with `about: access`) that no later yes to `access` has settled. `why` then quotes each with the owner's words and names the access commands and `semlayer readback --targets access`.

An item counts as answered when the owner's latest reply to its target, from any readback, has a verdict and the content hash in question; a later reply without a verdict opens it again. Steps 1, 2, 4 and 5 all read this one rule.

The first that applies, in this order:

0. No `semantic.yaml` at the root: `ask_owner`, phase `init`, `action` `semlayer init --name <name>`, with `ask_owner.text` asking the owner for a read-only connection and proposing a default `<name>`, and `why` saying what `<name>` takes, that `semlayer setup --name <name>` creates the layer and its first source in one command once the connection is named, and that `--root <dir>` reaches a layer elsewhere. `semlayer next` exits 0; `semlayer status` and `semlayer validate` refuse with `{ error, code, next }`, `code` `no_layer`.
1. A target whose owner's latest reply is a yes with the content hash it now has and that is not approved as it stands: `run` `semlayer approve --target <t>`, or for `golden:<g>` one not confirmed with those rows, `semlayer goldens confirm --name <g>`. When semlayer cannot run the golden's source (`runnable` false in `semlayer status`), it takes `--plan <plan_id>` of a stored plan of `semlayer goldens run --name <g>` that has every result; with no such plan stored, the action is `semlayer goldens run --name <g> --emit-sql`, and while one waits on results the golden is skipped so step 3 dispatches it. A golden whose source is an export source is skipped, since its confirm refuses (decision D2): the yes stays in the ledger for after the upgrade, and `semlayer status`'s `connect` step names that upgrade. Targets are taken in the order of those replies. When two or more targets of the first one's readback (goldens aside) wait so and none of its other yeses would be refused, the action is one `semlayer approve --readback <id>`. An action that names one typed edit semlayer apply takes says in `why` that it goes in the turn's one `semlayer apply --file -`, giving the entry (`["add-metric", "--name", ...]`) beside the command on its own; so do the refusals of a typed edit or `semlayer check-rule` that read an ignored column, with the `ignore-column ... --remove` entry.
2. The oldest readback in `.semlayer/pending/` with an open item: one whose target still has the content hash it was read back with and that is not answered at that hash: `ask_owner`. `why` says a readback the owner will not answer is set aside with `semlayer readback --discard <readback_id>`, quotes the owner's words for each item of it they already answered no or change to, and says that a change only to who sees an item is recorded with `--verdict change --about access` and never holds the item; an open item whose who-sees the owner already asked to change is named as kept for the access phase.
3. The first stored plan, by plan id, with a query that has no result: `dispatch`, with `action` `semlayer results status --plan <plan_id>`. A plan nobody needs any more is removed by deleting `.semlayer/plans/<plan_id>.json`.
4. `semlayer status`'s `next_step` as `run`: its command, phase and message. A `semlayer readback --targets` step leaves out the targets whose latest reply is a no or a change at the hash they now have, and names them in `why` with the owner's words quoted as data; a change followed by an `--about access` reply to the same content was only about who sees it, and the target is read back again. When none is left the action is `ask_owner`, `semlayer set <first of them> <key> <value>`: which typed edit the owner's words ask for is settled with the owner, and `why` names the two ways out, a typed edit to the item, or, when the change is only about who sees it, `semlayer reply` on that readback item with `--verdict change --about access`, followed by the owner's answer to the item itself. An `--emit-sql` step whose stored plan (its `finish` is the step's command with `--plan <plan_id>` in place of `--emit-sql`) has every result runs that `finish`, except a draft golden's `semlayer goldens run --name <g> --emit-sql`, which becomes `semlayer readback --targets golden:<g> --plan <plan_id>`.
5. At `done`, the first 8 of `approved_without_yes` that are not answered at the hash they now have (a golden: while its file exists, any hash), goldens left for last and read back one at a time: `run` `semlayer readback --targets <them>` (for a golden whose source semlayer cannot run, with `--plan` of its stored plan that has every result, or, with none stored, `semlayer goldens run --name <g> --emit-sql`), phase `done`. They never hold `done` back otherwise.
6. `done`, with `semlayer status`'s message.

A `run` `semlayer readback --targets` action from any step above whose targets `semlayer readback` would refuse for what their files lack (a description, a grain, a `primary_key`, a metric's measure; the check `semlayer approve` makes) becomes `ask_owner` `semlayer apply --file -` in the same phase, `why` naming each item, what it lacks and the edits to pass on standard input with a `<placeholder>` for each of the owner's words, `action.stdin` holding those edits, and `ask_owner.text` the numbered questions a person can be asked for them (what an item means, what one row is, which tag a field takes), so the command `semlayer next` names always runs.

Who sees an item is decided once, in the access phase, so open `access_changes` never hold another phase. An action of phase `access` carries them. When `access.yaml` is approved and step 4, 5 or 6 would give phase `verify` or `done`, they reopen the access phase instead: `run` `semlayer access`, phase `access`, and the first access edit makes the file a draft again, which `semlayer status` then holds at `access` until the owner's yes to its readback.

### Setup

`semlayer setup` brings one source from nothing, or from where it is, to a previewed scope (`scripts/commands/setup.ts`):

1. It runs `semlayer init` when the semantic root has no `semantic.yaml`, naming the project `--name`, else after the root's directory (its parent's when the root is `semantic`).
2. It writes `sources/<s>.yaml` with `intake.mode`, or edits it in place, every other key and comment as it was: `--env` sets `connection.env` and `--env-prefix` sets `connection.env_prefix`, each removing the other; `--database` and `--view` set a duckdb source's `connection.database` (`:memory:` for a new source without it) and replace `connection.views`; `--schemas` replaces `intake.schemas`; `--include`, `--exclude` and `--tables-file` together replace `scope` (`--tables-file`, read from the working directory, is copied to `scope/<s>.txt` and named in `scope.tables_file`, as `semlayer scope --from-file --write` does; a glob that differs from the file's only in case keeps the file's spelling). A flag group left out leaves its part of the file as it is. A rerun takes the source's connection flags again; without them it names them and `semlayer scope --write`.
3. Export mode (`--export <file>`): the export is copied to `exports/<s>.<ext>` under the root, and the file gets `intake.mode: export`, `intake.reason` from `--reason` (credentials redacted; without it a recorded reason stays, and a source with none gets an empty one and a warning) and `intake.export.file`. The export path takes no `--env`, `--database` or `--view`.
4. It reads the catalog when the source is new, has no snapshot, or its connection, `intake.mode`, `intake.schemas` or export changed: `semlayer introspect` limited to `intake.schemas`, or `semlayer import` of the export copy. A scope change alone reads nothing. A snowflake, bigquery or databricks source is not read: the next action is the query-plan step, `semlayer introspect --source <s> --emit-sql`.
5. It prints the scope preview (tables in scope of the snapshot's, by schema, the tables that entered or left scope, unmatched patterns, the focus threshold) and one next action: the query-plan step; `semlayer tables --source <s> --all` when no table is in scope; `semlayer tables --source <s> --groups` above the focus threshold; `semlayer profile --source <s>` while an in-scope table of a connection source is not profiled; else `semlayer baseline --source <s>`.

Every flag is checked before anything is written; a postgres source whose connection variables nothing sets (see Connection variables and env files) when the catalog must be read is refused with the files looked at and the line to add, writing nothing under the root: only the env-file lines go into the `.gitignore` beside it, ahead of the owner's `.env`. When reading the catalog fails, the source file, the copies and the snapshot are put back as they were; a project the run created stays. The source file and the copies are refused when their path leads outside the root through a link. The same arguments again change no file and say so. It refuses to change a source's dialect and to turn a connection source into an export source; an export source given its connection is upgraded (below).

#### Upgrade from an export

`semlayer setup --source <s> --dialect <d> --env VAR` (for duckdb, `--database` or `--view`) on a source whose `intake.mode` is `export` upgrades it to a connection without redoing the interview (`scripts/lib/upgrade.ts`):

1. The source file gets `intake.mode: connection` and the connection, keeping `intake.schemas`, `scope` and the table-list file. `intake.reason` and `intake.export` are removed, since they describe an export the source no longer reads (`source.changes` reports `reason: true` and `export_file: "removed"`); the export copy under `exports/` stays on disk and version control keeps the reason. Comments on the removed keys go with them; every other comment stays. A duckdb source without `--database` gets `connection.database: ":memory:"`.
2. `semlayer introspect` reads the catalog limited to `intake.schemas`, and its change report against the exported snapshot (added, removed and retyped columns, tables added and removed, tables new in scope and tables that left it; see Scope changes) is `changes`.
3. `semlayer profile` profiles the in-scope tables (`profiled`, their qualified names), unless no table or more than the focus threshold are in scope, and `semlayer check-join --all` measures every join and writes its evidence (`joins_checked`, as `source.model.join`; `joins_skipped`, `{ ref, reason }`, the joins it could not resolve or measure).
4. It lists the items the data contradicts, `contradictions: [{ ref, kind, evidence, approved }]`, sorted by `ref` and `kind`, `ref` being an approve target and `evidence` a plain sentence (data values in it are quoted and cut to 40 characters, at most five of them):
   - `primary_key_not_unique` (the model): a one-column `primary_key` that is null in some rows or has fewer distinct values than rows (beyond 1% when the profile was sampled or the dialect's distinct counts are approximate), or a join to the model on exactly its key columns whose evidence, measured for its current `to` and keys, found the key not unique;
   - `join_fans_out` (the join): a `many_to_one` or `one_to_one` join without `fanout_override` whose evidence, measured for its current `to` and keys, finds the target key not unique or a row matching several target rows;
   - `unlisted_value` (the dimension on the column, else the measure whose filter lists values): a status-like column, one of the model's own (not reached through a join) with at most 20 distinct values whose profile keeps top values (a protected column keeps none and is never judged), for which the owner listed values, as single-quoted literals a measure filter compares it with (`=`, `<>`, `!=`, `in`, `not in`) or in double quotes or backticks in a dimension's description (a quoted name of one of the table's columns or the model's fields is not a value), holding a top value that is neither listed nor named as a word (case, `_` and `-` folded) in the model's description, a dimension on the column's description or a filtering measure's description. A column no filter or description lists values for is not judged;
   - `column_not_in_export` (the model): the live table has columns the exported table did not show, other than its `ignored_columns`.
5. The next action is `semlayer readback --targets <the contradicted refs>`, else as for any connection source.

The upgrade never writes an approval field or the ledger: `.semlayer/ledger.jsonl` and every `approved_hash` stay as they were, and join evidence is not part of any hash. A contradicted approved item (`approved: true`) stays approved until the owner answers its readback: a change they agree to (a primary key, a relationship, a filter) changes the item's content, so it no longer matches its `approved_hash` and is a draft until approved again (see Approval hashes); where only the evidence contradicts it, the readback is the route back, and `semlayer validate` errors on an approved to-one join that fans out until the owner answers. A failure while reading, profiling or checking joins puts the source file, the snapshot and the model files back. A rerun of the same command after the upgrade is an ordinary connection setup and, with the same arguments, changes nothing. A snowflake, bigquery or databricks source is switched but not read: the next action is the query-plan step, and stays so on a rerun while the snapshot is not one introspect wrote (`method` other than `live`), and `profiled`, `joins_checked` and `contradictions` stay empty. The contradiction list is printed by the upgrade run only; a rerun does not list it again.

A connection source read by `semlayer introspect` adds `role_report` to `--json` (see The role report), and `warnings` gains its warnings and a note naming `semlayer setup --print-role-sql`.

#### Printing a read-only role

`semlayer setup --print-role-sql --dialect <d> --schemas a,b [--role-name <name>]` prints the statements for a least-privilege, read-only role limited to those schemas (`scripts/lib/role-sql.ts`). It needs no project, writes nothing, and never connects to a database or runs them; the owner's administrator does. Every name (`--schemas`, `--role-name`, default `semlayer_reader`, `SEMLAYER_READER` on Snowflake, `--database`, `--warehouse`, `--catalog`) must be a plain identifier (letters, digits and underscores, not starting with a digit), or it is refused, naming the flag but not echoing the value; names are quoted the way the dialect quotes (`"name"`, or `` `name` `` on BigQuery and Databricks). It takes no other `semlayer setup` flag. Per dialect:

- `postgres`: `CREATE ROLE "<role>" LOGIN PASSWORD '<password>'`, `GRANT CONNECT ON DATABASE` when `--database` is given, then per schema `GRANT USAGE ON SCHEMA`, `GRANT SELECT ON ALL TABLES IN SCHEMA` and `ALTER DEFAULT PRIVILEGES IN SCHEMA ... GRANT SELECT ON TABLES`, and `ALTER ROLE ... SET default_transaction_read_only = on`; comments say to replace `<password>` (in an environment variable or the project's env file, never in the layer's files or the chat), how default privileges apply to tables another role creates, and the `REVOKE CREATE ON SCHEMA public FROM PUBLIC` for Postgres 14 or earlier.
- `redshift`: the same grants for `CREATE USER "<role>" PASSWORD '<password>'`, since a Redshift role cannot log in, without the `ALTER ROLE` line.
- `snowflake` (needs `--database` and `--warehouse`): `CREATE ROLE IF NOT EXISTS`, `USAGE` on the warehouse, the database and each schema, `SELECT` on all and future tables and views of each schema, and `GRANT ROLE ... TO USER <user>`. Quoted names match their exact case, as the comments say; the default role is upper case, so an unquoted `USE ROLE SEMLAYER_READER` finds it.
- `bigquery` (needs `--project`; `--schemas` names the datasets): shell commands, since the project-level grant has no SQL form: `gcloud iam service-accounts create` for the role name with `_` written as `-` (6 to 30 characters), `gcloud projects add-iam-policy-binding` with `roles/bigquery.jobUser`, and per dataset a `bq query` running `GRANT roles/bigquery.dataViewer ON SCHEMA`; a comment says to add `--location=<region>` for datasets outside the US multi-region.
- `databricks` (needs `--catalog`): `GRANT USE CATALOG`, then per schema `GRANT USE SCHEMA` and `GRANT SELECT ON SCHEMA`, to a group or service principal of that name.

The output ends with the next step: the `semlayer setup ... --env <VAR> --schemas a,b` that connects once the role exists (for Redshift, the warehouse connector or `semlayer export-sql --dialect redshift`). `--json` has `language` (`sql`, or `shell` for BigQuery), `statements` (each runnable line), `text` (the whole output with its comments) and `placeholders` (`<password>`, `<user>`), each for the owner to fill in.

#### The role report

On connect, the Postgres adapter measures what the role can do with two read-only catalog queries, inside the same read-only transaction as every query (`scripts/lib/adapters/postgres.ts`): `{ dialect, role, superuser, write_privileges: [{ schema, privileges }], server_file_roles, readable_schemas, beyond_intake, least_privilege, schemas_measured, warnings }`. `write_privileges` lists, per schema outside the system schemas, `INSERT`, `UPDATE`, `DELETE` or `TRUNCATE` on any of its tables (`INSERT` and `UPDATE` on any one column count) and `CREATE` on the schema; `server_file_roles` the memberships of `pg_read_server_files`, `pg_write_server_files` and `pg_execute_server_program`; `readable_schemas` the schemas the role may use and read a table or view of, or any one column of one (not every schema it has `USAGE` on, so `public` with no readable table is not reach); `beyond_intake` those outside `intake.schemas`, `null` when it lists none. The first query proves the connection; when the per-schema one fails (a timeout on a large catalog), the command goes on with `schemas_measured: false`, empty `write_privileges` and `readable_schemas`, `beyond_intake: null` and a warning that the role was not measured. `least_privilege` is true when the role is no superuser, its schemas were measured and none of the others lists anything. `warnings` holds the one warning printed on stderr: for a superuser, "connects as a superuser. Queries run read-only, but connect with a SELECT-only role."; otherwise one sentence naming what it can do and the `semlayer setup --print-role-sql` that prints a narrower role. `semlayer setup --json` and `semlayer introspect --json` add it as `role_report`; the plain output of both adds a line telling the agent to pass the warning to the owner. It never refuses and never changes the read-only guarantees.

**Relations the role cannot read.** On Postgres, `semlayer introspect` lists a relation (table, partitioned table, view, materialized view or foreign table) only when the role has SELECT on it or on any one of its columns (`has_any_column_privilege`, the test the role report uses). The snapshot holds no table, column or key for any other relation, and a foreign key into one is left out. A live run prints one line naming what it skipped, `Skipped 2 relations this role cannot read: acme.a, acme.b.`, with the grant to ask for and the command to rerun, and `--json` carries them in `skipped_unreadable: [{ schema, name }]`. A run from a results file or plan reads no list, and a column row with no matching `tables` row is dropped. `semlayer export-sql --dialect postgres` applies the same test. A partitioned table is one entry (its partitions are left out), a foreign table has kind `external`, and a view or materialized view has kind `view` and no `row_count` until `semlayer profile` measures it; a name in mixed case is kept as the catalog spells it and quoted wherever it is written into SQL.

### Demo project

`semlayer demo [--dir <dir>]` makes a sample project to try semlayer on, with synthetic data and no warehouse (`scripts/commands/demo.ts`, `scripts/lib/demo.ts`). It finds the plugin from its own script's real path, never the working directory, and reads `fixtures/acme/data` and `fixtures/acme/reference-figures.json` there; a copy of the skill directory alone has neither, and the refusal names the repository to clone. In `<dir>` it writes:

- `data/`: every file under `fixtures/acme/data`, byte for byte, the clutter tables included, so the scope step has something to do.
- `semantic/`: `semlayer init --name acme_demo`, then `semlayer setup --source acme --dialect duckdb` with one `--view` per warehouse CSV (`read_csv('../data/warehouse/<t>.csv')`) and per lake dataset (`read_parquet('../data/lake/<t>.parquet')`, a folder of files as `<t>/*.parquet`). The paths are relative to the root, so the folder can be moved or committed. One source holds all 25 tables, more than the focus threshold, so `semlayer next` names the scope step first.
- `OWNER.md`: a cheat sheet for the person who plays the data owner: the tables that matter, the business rules, who may see what, and the 2025 figures from `reference-figures.json` with their definitions and what a missed rule gives. It says every value is synthetic. The agent never reads it; the owner's answers come only from the owner. In a root whose project is `acme_demo` with `OWNER.md` beside it, `semlayer next` ends its `why`, and `semlayer status` its next step's `message`, with "This is the demo project: OWNER.md belongs to the person playing the owner; do not read it, and take every answer from the owner."
- `.gitignore`, as `semlayer setup` writes it. No env file.

The folder is built beside `<dir>` under a hidden name and renamed into place, so a failure leaves nothing. The same command in another folder gives the same files: the snapshot's `captured_at` is `SEMLAYER_NOW` when it is set and `2026-01-01T00:00:00.000Z`, the day after the data ends, otherwise. A refusal with `--json` prints `{ error, code, next }`. The plain output is at most eight lines: what it made, and the next step (`cd <dir> && claude`, then type `/semlayer` or ask for a semantic layer for the data in the folder).

### Typed edits

`set`, `tag`, `ignore-column`, `question add`, `add-field`, `remove-field`, `add-join`, `add-bridge`, `remove-join`, `add-segment`, `remove-segment`, `add-metric`, `remove-metric` and the access edits (`add-attribute`, `add-role`, `add-restriction`, `add-row-filter` and their `remove-*` commands; see Access edits) each change one YAML file, and `rename` changes every file that names the item; each file stays byte-identical outside what they change, comments and approved items included. Each one:

- validates the project as `semlayer validate` would read it with the edit, every file it changes read together, once (the project as it was is read only to tell an error the edit adds from one already there), and writes nothing, in any file, exiting 1 and listing the errors, when the edit adds an error `semlayer validate` does not already report; that an approved item changed since its approval is not one of them (below);
- writes nothing and reports `changed: false` when the file already says what it asks for, so running it again is safe (`add-field`, `remove-field`, `add-join`, `add-bridge`, `remove-join`, `add-segment`, `remove-segment`, `add-metric`, `remove-metric` and the access `add-*` and `remove-*` commands refuse instead: a name already taken, an item already gone; the same row filter again changes nothing);
- writes only a file that resolves inside the semantic root, and refuses a model file or directory linked to a path outside it;
- folds owner text (`--reason`, `--text`, `add-field` and `add-metric` `--description`, or `--set description=`) to one line without control characters, so it adds no key or comment line;
- prints every warning `semlayer validate` gives that the edit adds, as `Warning: <file> <path>: <message>` before its `Next:` line, and with `--json` as lines `"<file> <path>: <message>"` in its `warnings` list (added to the list a command already prints) and as `{ code, file, path, message }` objects in its `warning_issues` list; the once-per-file counts of approved items without a hash or a matching owner yes are not reported, since only `semlayer approve` adds to them;
- refuses, with `semlayer validate`'s own words and nothing written, an edit that adds a warning about a value that can never be right: a `time_zone` on a time dimension whose column carries its own zone, a measure `expr` that divides by a numeric literal on duckdb ("this warning never stands"), and a column in `ignored_columns` that a field, a filter, a join key or a row filter reads (see Ignored columns in typed edits); the access edits also refuse a restriction target that matches nothing and an `allow.tags` entry that is not a protected tag (see Access edits).

An edit that changes the content an approval hash covers (see Approval hashes) of approved items, its own target or fields that read through an edited join, in any model, lists them in `reapprove`, unless it restores the content an item's `approved_hash` records, and asks for each to be read back to the owner as it now stands and approved again only on their yes (`semlayer approve --target <ref> --by "<owner>"`). Until then `semlayer validate` errors on each that records an `approved_hash`, `semlayer status` routes to `reapprove`, and tools treat each as a draft (see Approval hashes). An approved item in a project approved before hashes applies as it stands, so an edit that would loosen what an approved item lets people see takes effect only after a revoke (see `tag` and `set`), and an edit of an approved `access.yaml`'s rules writes the file as a draft (see Access edits). Typed edits write no other approval field.

`set` also takes three targets `approve` does not:

- `set access mode open|closed` with no `access.yaml` creates one holding `mode` and `status: draft`, with no comments, and prints what it wrote and the next steps (`semlayer access`, then the readback). It validates the project with the new file first and writes nothing when that adds an error (a golden whose `role` or `user` the new file does not declare, which `set golden:<g> role --unset` removes; in closed mode, a confirmed golden that names what nobody may see until roles are added, so it suggests `set access mode open`), and never writes over a file or a link already at the path. Any other `set access` with no file is refused, naming it.
- `set golden:<g> <key>` takes `role` (a role name), `user` (a JSON object of user attributes, without control characters), `reference` (folded to one line, as owner text is) and `tolerance` (a decimal from 0 up to 1, as `goldens confirm --tolerance` takes it, written as `expected.tolerance`; on a draft with no `expected`, as `expected: { tolerance, rows: [] }`, which `--unset` removes again). It refuses the confirmation fields (`status`, `confirmed_by`, `confirmed_at`, `confirmed_as`), naming `semlayer goldens confirm`, and any other key, listing the four, and a name that is not lower-case letters, digits and `_`. It refuses setting `role` or `user` while there is no `access.yaml`, naming `set access mode`. On a confirmed golden (`status: approved`) it refuses `tolerance`, which only `goldens confirm` records with the rows, naming `semlayer readback --targets golden:<g> --tolerance <t>` (with no `expected.tolerance`, the default 0.005 is no change); a change to `role` or `user`, which the golden's content hash covers, is written and listed in `reapprove` as `golden:<g>` unless a recorded owner yes matches the golden as it then stands, and the output asks for `semlayer goldens check --name <g>`, `semlayer readback --targets golden:<g>`, `semlayer reply` and `semlayer goldens confirm --name <g>` (until then `semlayer validate` warns, as for a hand edit). `reference` is outside the hash.
- `set source:<s> description <text>` writes the source file's `description`, folded to one line without control characters, after `dialect`; any other key is refused, listing `description`.

#### Ignored columns in typed edits

A column in a model's `ignored_columns` was left out of the layer on purpose. Every command that writes or checks an expression or a join key refuses one that reads such a column, the same way, naming `semlayer ignore-column --source <s> --model <m> --column <c> --remove` for each, which takes it back out after the owner confirms: `add-field` (its `expr` and `filter`), `set` on a dimension's or measure's `expr` or `filter` and on a join's `keys` or `to` (a column the old value already read is not refused again), `add-row-filter` (its `expr`), `add-join` (its key columns, on both models) and `check-rule` (before any query). An expression reads a column on its own model, or through its joins (`<join>.<column>`, `<join>.<join>.<column>`) on the model they reach, as the compiler qualifies it. `add-field --unignore` still takes the model's own column back in the same edit. The other way round, `ignore-column` refuses a column the layer reads: one a field, a join key, the primary key or another model's join reads on the model itself, and one another model's field reads through a join or a row filter of `access.yaml` reads (`semlayer validate` warns on each such entry, naming `ignore-column --remove`). `semlayer status` counts no such column among a model's new columns, and `draft --new-columns` leaves it alone: it is in use, and its values stay protected by the sensitivity rules.

#### Access edits

Every rule of `access.yaml` has a typed command, so the file is never written by hand:

| Rule | Commands |
|---|---|
| The mode and the default role | `set access mode open\|closed`, `set access default_role <role>` |
| User attributes | `add-attribute`, `remove-attribute`, `set access attribute:<name> <key>` (`type`, `description`) |
| Roles | `add-role`, `remove-role`, `set access role:<name> <key>` (`description`, `assign_when`, `allow.models`, `allow.tags`, `deny.models`, `deny.fields`, `deny.tags`) |
| A role's row filters | `add-row-filter`, `remove-row-filter` |
| Restrictions | `add-restriction`, `remove-restriction`, `set access restriction:<name> <key>` (`description`, `targets`, `allow.roles`, `allow.when`) |
| Open questions | `question add access`, `question add access.roles.<role>`, `question resolve` |

Each is a typed edit as above (validated with the project, comments kept, the root's lock held, nothing written on an error), and also:

- refuses with `access.yaml` missing, naming `semlayer set access mode open` (or `closed`), which creates it;
- takes a list value as `a,b` (or `[a, b]`), written on one line, and a description folded to one line; a `set access <kind>:<name>` key that already holds the value changes nothing, `--unset` removes a key, the last key of `allow` or `deny` taking the map with it, and a key the entry needs (`type`, `targets`, the last of `allow.roles` and `allow.when`) is not removed;
- puts a new section where the file's order has it (`mode`, `default_role`, `user_attributes`, `roles`, `restrictions`, `open_questions`) and a new key likewise (`name`, `description`, `assign_when`, `allow`, `deny`, `row_filters`, `open_questions` on a role);
- refuses a name already taken, and a removal while anything names what it removes, naming each with the command that changes it: a role named by `default_role`, a restriction's `allow.roles` or a golden's `role`; an attribute read by an `assign_when`, an `allow.when`, a row filter or a golden's `user`;
- refuses what `semlayer validate` would report as an error with the edit (a condition that does not parse or reads an undeclared attribute, an unknown role in `allow.roles`, a row filter on a model that does not exist, a join reference that cannot be followed, a hierarchy whose closure model or columns do not exist), and besides that a restriction target that matches nothing and a row filter `expr` naming a column the model's table lacks in the snapshot;
- prints the warnings `semlayer validate` gives that the edit added (`--json`: `warnings`, each as `<path>: <message>`), such as a child table a new row filter does not limit;
- makes an approved `access.yaml` a draft again in the same write, exactly as `semlayer approve --target access --revoke` followed by the edit would leave it: `status: draft`, `approved_by` and `approved_at` moved into `last_approved`, `approved_hash` removed. Tools apply an approved file as it stands, so the edit takes effect only once the owner approves the file again; the output says so, names `semlayer readback --targets access`, and `--json` adds `returned_to_draft: true` and `last_approved`. `semlayer set access mode` and `semlayer set access default_role` do the same. An open question is outside the approval hash and leaves the file approved.

#### Batches of edits

`semlayer apply --file <path>` (or `--file -` for stdin) runs a list of typed edits, `{"edits": [["set", "<ref>", "<key>", "<value>"], ["tag", "<ref>", "pii"], ...]}`, each entry the words of one command after `semlayer`. It takes the root's lock once and:

- refuses the whole list, writing nothing, when an entry is not one of `set`, `tag`, `ignore-column`, `question add`, `add-field`, `remove-field`, `rename`, `add-metric`, `remove-metric`, `add-join`, `add-bridge`, `remove-join`, `add-segment`, `remove-segment`, `goldens new` (it writes a draft golden and compiles its request, querying nothing), `goldens unconfirm`, `goldens remove` and the access edits, naming the entry: one that records an owner answer or approval (`reply`, `approve`, `goldens confirm`, `question resolve`, `add-metric --none`) or reads a database (`profile`, `readback`, `check-join` and the rest) runs on its own; an entry also passes no `--root`, `--env-file`, `--json` or `--help`, which `apply` gives every entry itself;
- runs the entries in order, each seeing the files as the entries before it left them, and each with its own checks and refusals; it validates the project once, after the last, against the project as it was before the first (as one typed edit does);
- checks every entry before it gives up: an entry that is refused (not a typed edit, does not parse, or its own checks refuse it) is skipped and the rest still run, so one refusal names every refused entry, each with its number, its command and its own message; an entry that names what a refused entry would have created (as a whole word, a dotted ref, a `metric:`, `role:` or `golden:` form or a `{model.measure}` placeholder; a word inside free text such as a description does not count) is listed as not checked rather than with the error that follows from the first, and the heading counts both;
- on any failure leaves every file under the root as it was, byte for byte, and exits 1 with those messages (one refused entry alone with its own message); when the final validation finds an error, the entries are tried again one by one, each validated on its own, to name each entry that adds an error the batch ends with (an entry whose error a later entry fixes runs on), and an error the batch ends with that no entry adds is listed under the refusal;
- prints each entry's own result line, then the approved items the batch changed and an approved `access.yaml` it made a draft, once, as they stand at its end, with `semlayer readback --targets` for them.

Each entry writes its files as it runs, each file whole, and a failure puts them back; a process killed in the middle of a batch can leave the entries before it written, which `semlayer validate` and `semlayer status` show.

A role that runs only some commands has each entry checked as if it ran it on its own, and passes `--file <path>`.

#### Renaming a model

`semlayer rename <source.model> <new_name>` writes the model file's text, its `model` key changed and every other byte kept, to `models/<source>/<new_name>.yaml`, and removes the old file, in the same validated write as the references: `to` and `evidence.to` of every join to it in the source's models; `model.measure` in the `numerator`, `denominator` and `expr` of the source's metrics; `model.field` (keeping a `:grain`) in the requests of goldens in its source; and in `access.yaml`, row filters' `model` and `hierarchy.via`, and the entries of `allow.models`, `deny.models`, `deny.fields` and restriction targets whose first two parts are the source and the model (`warehouse.orders`, `warehouse.orders.*`), the rest kept as written. It refuses a name another model of the source has, something already at the new path (a file or a link), and a rename that would change whether one of those `access.yaml` lists covers the model or one of its fields, read as `semlayer access` reads it, naming each entry; open questions stay as written. An approval covers the model's source and name, so every approved item of the model, every approved join to it and field reading through one, every approved metric and an approved `access.yaml` whose content it changes go to `reapprove`: tools treat them as drafts until the owner approves them again, and the output names one `semlayer readback --targets` for all of them. It names the confirmed goldens whose request changed, for `semlayer goldens check`, a readback and `semlayer goldens confirm`. Renaming it back restores every file and with them every approval.

### Hand edits

The Claude Code plugin (`.claude-plugin/plugin.json`) registers a `PreToolUse` hook, `scripts/hooks/guard-edits.ts`, on `Edit`, `MultiEdit`, `Write`, `NotebookEdit`, `Bash`, `Read` and `Grep` (`Read` and `Grep` for the env file alone, below). It reads the call from stdin, resolves the target path (relative to the call's `cwd`, links followed) and finds its semantic root, the nearest directory at or above it holding `semantic.yaml`. It refuses the call, exiting 2 with the reason and the command to use instead on stderr, when the call would:

- add, change or remove an approval field (`status: approved`, `approved_by`, `approved_at`, `approved_hash`, `last_approved`, `confirmed_by`, `confirmed_at`, `confirmed_as`) anywhere in `models/`, `metrics.yaml`, `access.yaml`, `goldens/` or the schema pairs of `sources/<s>.yaml`: only `semlayer approve` and `semlayer goldens confirm` write them, on the owner's recorded yes;
- change what the content hash of an approved model, dimension, measure, join, metric or schema pair, or of an approved `access.yaml`, covers (see Approval hashes), or remove one; for a pair the refusal names `semlayer add-cross-schema` and `semlayer remove-cross-schema`, which send it back to draft. That includes a join in another model that an approved field reads through, and the `model` and `source` keys of a model file that holds approved items. A change that restores the content its `approved_hash` records is allowed. Use a typed edit, which routes the item to re-approval (for `access.yaml`, an access edit, which makes it a draft again), or `semlayer approve --target <ref> --revoke` first;
- change a confirmed golden's `expected` rows or tolerance: only `semlayer goldens confirm` records them;
- write `.semlayer/ledger.jsonl` or anything under `.semlayer/pending/`;
- write `changelog.yaml`: it is append-only and only `semlayer change close` adds to it;
- write `.semlayer/change.json`: only `semlayer change start` writes it and `semlayer change close` removes it.

`Edit` and `MultiEdit` are checked on the file as their edits would leave it, matched as the Edit tool matches (also across CRLF line endings and curly quotes). Everything else is allowed: drafts and every field of them, descriptions and open questions of approved items (outside the hash), a confirmed golden's question, request, sql, role, user and reference (`semlayer validate` warns until an owner yes matches it again), `semantic.yaml`, everything in `sources/` but the approval of its schema pairs, `snapshots/`, `.semlayer/plans/`, and every file outside a semantic root.

For `Bash`, the hook reads the command as a shell splits it (quotes, escapes, heredocs, brace expansion, `$(...)` and backquoted commands, `sh -c` and `bash -ec` strings, a heredoc or `<<<` string given to a shell, `eval`, and the options of `sudo`, `env`, `xargs` and `timeout`), finds each command's name past the shell keywords before it (`if`, `then`, `else`, `elif`, `while`, `until`, `do`, `{`, `!`, and a function body: `if true; then rm models/wh/orders.yaml; fi`), and refuses any plain write to `models/`, `goldens/`, `metrics.yaml`, `access.yaml`, `changelog.yaml`, a `sources/<s>.yaml` that holds approval fields, `.semlayer/ledger.jsonl`, `.semlayer/pending/` or the root itself: a redirection (`>`, `>>`, `&>`, ...), `tee`, `sed -i`, `perl -i`, `yq -i`, `awk -i inplace`, `cp`, `mv`, `install`, `ln`, `rsync`, `patch`, `rm`, `unlink`, `shred`, `truncate` or `dd of=`, with globs expanded against the files that exist. A relative path counts against the call's `cwd` and against every directory a `cd` or `pushd` in the command names, since a subshell, `cd -` or `popd` may return to any of them; this can refuse a command that writes elsewhere. Removing or moving a root's `semantic.yaml` (`rm`, `unlink`, `mv`) is refused too, since it would leave the root unguarded. A shell write cannot be compared item by item, so it is refused for drafts too; edit a draft with the Edit tool. A path built from a variable is refused only when it names the ledger or `.semlayer/pending/`. `semlayer` commands, reads and writes elsewhere are allowed.

A hook sees only what a tool call states. A program that writes files itself (a script, `python -c`, `find -exec`, `git checkout`, `git apply`, an editor), commands piped into a shell (`... | sh`), files `xargs` reads from its input, files a diff names when `patch` is given none, a write to `models/` or `changelog.yaml` through a variable path, a file named in another case or spelling such as `CHANGELOG.yaml`, and the removal of a directory that holds a root are not seen by these checks. What such a program changes in the answer ledger and the pending readbacks is put back by the comparison below. For the layer's YAML the CLI's checks remain the guarantee: `semlayer validate` reports an approved item whose content no longer matches its `approved_hash` and an approval without a matching ledger yes, and `semlayer approve` and `semlayer goldens confirm` approve nothing without one.

Input that names no file (not JSON, or no `tool_name`, `tool_input`, path or command) is let through with a warning on stderr, since there is nothing to check. For a file inside a semantic root, an edit the hook cannot apply (an `old_string` not in the file, a missing `content`), YAML that would not parse in a file that holds approval fields, and an error while checking are refused. A file with approval fields that does not parse now (a merge conflict, say) may be repaired with text that parses when every approved item in it matches the `approved_hash` it records, a hash the old text holds, and no approval field appears more often than in the old text; otherwise, and for a confirmed golden, the refusal names `git checkout -- <file>` or the owner. These checks write nothing; the hook needs `bun` on `PATH` and the plugin's packages, which Claude Code installs from the plugin root's `package.json` and `bun.lock` (the `SessionStart` hook names the fallback, `bun install --frozen-lockfile --production` in the plugin root), as the CLI does.

**The ledger comparison.** `semlayer approve` rests on a yes in `.semlayer/ledger.jsonl`, so a line put there by anything but `semlayer reply` is a forged owner yes, and the checks above see only the writes a command states. The plugin therefore also compares. Before a `Bash` call the `PreToolUse` hook copies `.semlayer/ledger.jsonl` and the files of `.semlayer/pending/` of every semantic root it finds into its state directory. The roots are the one at or above the call's `cwd`, each directory a `cd` in the command names, `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` and every path the command text names; `semantic/` and the other roots directly inside those directories (among a directory's first 500 entries); and `SEMANTIC_ROOT`. The state directory is `${CLAUDE_PLUGIN_DATA}/ledger-guard`, or `semantic-layer-guard-<uid>` under the system temp directory when that variable is unset, readable by its user alone. A copy is keyed by the call's `tool_use_id`, or by its session, tool and input when it has none; the command text is not stored. After the call, `scripts/hooks/integrity.ts`, registered on `PostToolUse` and `PostToolUseFailure`, compares the files with the copy:

- nothing differs: the copy is removed;
- a file differs: it is put back (a changed or deleted file restored, an added one removed), and the hook exits 2 with `A command changed the answer ledger outside semlayer, and the change was undone. An owner's answer is recorded only with semlayer reply, after the owner answers.` and the files it put back. A file that was given a second name (a hard link, through which a later command could write it without naming it) counts as differing: the file put back is a new one, which that name no longer reaches. It writes only under the root's `.semlayer/` and never through a link that leads out of the root; a file it cannot put back is named with `git checkout -- <file>`;
- a file differs and `semlayer` or git ran at the same time: it is left, and the hook exits 2 saying that the change could not be attributed and to run `semlayer validate`.

A plain `semlayer` command and a git-only command get no copy; each records a marker that it is running, removed once no copy can overlap it. In a plain `semlayer` command every simple command is `bun [run] <semlayer.ts> ...` (the plugin's own `semlayer.ts` with its path written out) or a bare `semlayer ...` that is the plugin's own `bin/semlayer` (below), a git command as below, a `cd`, or one of `head`, `tail`, `grep`, `wc`, `cat`, `jq`, `cut`, `tr`, `echo`, `printf` (not `printf -v`, which sets a variable), `ls`, `pwd`, `test`, `[`, `sleep`, `true`, `false`, `exit` and `tee` (to files written out), which write only the files they name, and those the checks above have seen, and there is no `$(...)` or backquoted command, no variable set before a command other than `SEMLAYER_*` and `SEMANTIC_ROOT`, and no redirection to a path built from a variable. A git-only command is the same without `semlayer`: at least one of `git pull`, `merge`, `rebase`, `checkout`, `switch`, `restore`, `reset`, `revert`, `cherry-pick` and `stash`, which bring committed ledger lines into the working tree, and besides them only `git fetch`, `status`, `add`, `commit`, `log`, `diff`, `show`, `branch`, `push`, `remote`, `tag` and `rev-parse`, with `-C <dir>` the one option read before the subcommand, and with no `--output` option and no argument built from a variable. The MCP server's `semlayer_<command>` tools count as `semlayer`, and `integrity.ts` runs before and after them. Every other command is compared, so a `semlayer` or git write inside `semlayer reply ... && python3 report.py`, `$SEMLAYER reply ...` or `git pull && make` is put back with the rest. The report then says so, and names `semlayer reply`, `semlayer question resolve` and `semlayer review apply` as the commands that record an owner's answer. For a call that named `semlayer`: the ledger lines `semlayer` wrote were removed, a layer file it edited was not put back (after `semlayer question resolve` or `semlayer review apply`, a resolved question is gone from its file without its answer), and the way to record the answer is `semlayer question add` for such a question, then the `semlayer` command again on its own and `semlayer validate`. For a call that ran one of the git commands above: `git checkout -- <file>` for each file listed, on its own, gets back what a pull or merge brought in.

Markers and copies carry the agent that made the call (`session_id` and `agent_id`), since one agent's calls that write run one after another. At the start of every call the `PreToolUse` hooks see, a copy the same agent left behind is compared and settled the same way: its after-check never ran, because the session was interrupted or the Claude Code in use has no `PostToolUseFailure`. A difference found there stops the current call with the report, and the call passes when run again. A copy from another agent is compared too, and kept while it is unchanged and under ten minutes old, since its call may still be running. A marker with no end counts as over when the same agent makes its next call, and as running for ten minutes to any other agent. A command that leaves a program running (`&`, `nohup`, `setsid`, `disown`, or `run_in_background`) is compared once more at the next call. State older than 24 hours is removed without a comparison.

The comparison is a second check, not a sandbox. Not undone: a write by a program still running after that second look, a write to a root the hook did not find, a write made while `semlayer` or git ran, and a change that a later `semlayer` or git command makes or brings back because an earlier command arranged it (a commit or a stash holding the change, a git hook, a `bunfig.toml` preload). Put back along with the rest: a change another program makes to those files while a compared call runs, such as the owner running `semlayer reply` in a terminal. An error of the hook's own (a state directory it cannot write, a copy it cannot read) never refuses a call: it prints a warning on stderr and exits 0.

**Which command is semlayer.** The checks above trust a command only when it is this plugin's CLI. The full form `bun [run] <skill dir>/scripts/semlayer.ts` with the path written out always is. A bare `semlayer` is, only when the hook can show it runs the plugin's own `bin/semlayer` (`bareSemlayerReach` in `scripts/hooks/shell-writes.ts`, `bareSemlayer` in `scripts/lib/plugin-bin.ts`):

- the command text cannot reach another program by that name: it names no `PATH`, `BASH_CMDS` or `BASH_ALIASES`, in its raw text or in any word with quotes and escapes removed (`P\ATH` is `PATH`), runs no `alias`, `unalias`, `function`, `hash`, `source`, `.`, `eval`, `exec`, `env`, `command`, `builtin`, `enable`, `export`, `declare`, `typeset`, `readonly`, `local`, `set`, `shopt`, `unset`, `trap`, `read`, `mapfile`, `readarray`, `getopts` or `printf -v`, defines no function named `semlayer`, holds no `$(...)` or backquoted command, names no command by a variable, and sets no variable before a command but `SEMLAYER_*` and `SEMANTIC_ROOT`;
- along the `PATH` the hook process sees, with the plugin's `bin/` added last as Claude Code places it, the first executable `semlayer` is the plugin's `bin/semlayer` by real path (a link to it counts), and no relative or empty `PATH` entry comes before it;
- that file is the one this plugin version ships: a plain file (not a link) under the plugin root `CLAUDE_PLUGIN_ROOT` names, which must be the root the hooks run from, executable, with the SHA-256 `PLUGIN_BIN_SHA256` records.

When any of these fails, the bare `semlayer` is another program: a strict role agent's call is refused, and the comparison copies the ledger around the call and undoes what it wrote, saying why the name was not counted and naming both forms. Limits: the hook reads the command text and its own `PATH`, not the agent shell's; an alias or function the user's own shell profile defines, a `PATH` that profile sets differently, and a file swapped between the hook's look and the shell's run are not seen.

**The env file.** The hook refuses to read the env file a semantic root loads its connection from: `.env` and `.env.<name>` (not `.env.example`) in a semantic root or the directory that holds one, and the file `SEMLAYER_ENV_FILE` names. Before a semantic root exists it refuses the same names in the project directory (`CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`) and the call's working directory, once the project has shown it uses this plugin: a `semlayer` command ran there (this plugin's `semlayer.ts`, a command named `semlayer`, or one of the MCP server's `semlayer` tools), or a file under the plugin's `skills/semantic-layer` was read with `Read`, `Grep` or a shell command. The hook records that evidence in its state directory, one file per project directory (`project-<hash>`), and keeps it; a call that is itself evidence is checked as one (`semlayer next; cat .env` is refused); a project with no such evidence keeps its `.env` readable. It refuses the `Read` tool on it, and in `Bash` `cat`, `head`, `tail`, `less`, `more`, `bat`, `grep`, `egrep`, `fgrep`, `rg`, `sed`, `awk`, `gawk`, `source`, `.`, `tac`, `nl`, `od`, `xxd`, `strings`, `base64` and a `<` redirection, also after a shell keyword (`then`, `do`, `{`, `!`), in a function body and inside `$(...)`, `sh -c` strings and `eval`, with `This file holds the connection secret. semlayer reads it itself, and you never need its contents. To check the connection, run semlayer setup --source <s>.` A `grep -r` over the file's directory is refused too, unless its `--exclude` or `--include` patterns leave the file out, and that refusal adds `To search the other files, name a subdirectory or add --exclude='.env*'.` `.env.example`, a `.env` in a directory with no semantic root and no such evidence, a grep whose pattern is the file's name (`grep -e .env .gitignore`), and commands that only look for the file (`ls`, `test -f`) are allowed. A path built from a variable and a program that opens the file itself are not seen.

The `Grep` tool searches every file below its `path`, dot files included, so the hook refuses a `Grep` whose `path` is an env file, whatever its `glob` and `type`, and one whose `path` is a directory with an env file anywhere below it (the working directory when it gives none), unless its `glob` or `type` leaves that file out. That refusal adds `To search the other files, give a subdirectory as path, or a glob that leaves this file out (glob: "!.env")`, with the refused file's own name. A `glob` leaves the file out when it holds a pattern without `!` and none of them matches the file's name or path, or when a `!` pattern matches its name; a `type` leaves out a file named `.env`, which no ripgrep type matches, and not `.env.<name>`, which may end in an extension one does. The hook looks in the first 1,000 directories below `path`, nearest first, skipping `.git` and `node_modules`, and adds the file `SEMLAYER_ENV_FILE` names when it lies below; it reads no file. A `Grep` on a subdirectory with no env file below it, such as the semantic root when `.env` is beside it, is untouched. The `Glob` tool lists names only and is not hooked. An error of the hook's own while it looks for an env file, for any of these tools, prints a warning on stderr and leaves the call to the other checks.
